  - The analytics double-count is gone: Fridays Past correctly shows 15 bookings (was 21), Fifty Proof shows 5 (was ~11).
  - The preferred-artists "next gig date" query hits one DB path instead of five UNION legs — noticeable in the "My Artists" tab load time for venues with many preferred artists.
  - `list_artist_gigs_public` now does one DB round-trip instead of two + Python dedup.
  - Identity resolution in `messages.py` is one query instead of three-tier fallback.

  **Remaining known-dead-but-harmless sites** (won't sweep unless launch surfaces a specific bug):
  - `gigs.py` has ~20 more `LEFT JOIN artists a ON g.artist_id = a.id` display-only joins that produce correct output (they NULL-out for gigs where `gigs.artist_id` isn't set, and downstream code has always handled that). Safe to leave.
  - `gigs.py:4530` `list_artist_gigs` (auth'd) has `WHERE g.artist_id = :aid OR contract_hold_artist_id = :aid OR gs.artist_id ...` — the middle leg captures artists mid-contract-flow before the slot is booked. Leaving as-is because it protects an edge case.
  - `payout_scheduler.py:44-1499` slot-first-with-gig-fallback pay reads — the fallback is dead code post-backfill but the money-risk of touching this pre-launch outweighs the cleanup value.
  - `update_gig` at `gigs.py:3520` still writes gig-level `pay/start_time/end_time/artist_type/band_formats/styles` as dupes of slot data. `send_gig_edited_emails` still reads these as the umbrella. Freezing would require touching ~15 email-render + admin-query sites — deferred.

  All 4 test endpoints returned 200 (`/`, `/api/gigs/public`, `/app/venue-create-gigs.html`, `/app/artist-book-gigs.html`). Zero errors in logs. Services restarted clean. Ready to launch.

  **Phase 2d — Final two-query merges collapsed (July 6 same-day):** Cleaned the last two remaining classic `regular_gigs + slot_gigs + seen_ids` dedup patterns surfaced by the earlier grep sweep.
  1. **`artists.py:587-650 get_artist_venue_gigs`** (`/api/artists/{id}/venues/{vid}/gigs` — My Venues "gigs at this venue" section). Was two queries (single-slot via `g.artist_id`, multi-slot via `gs.artist_id`) then Python `seen_ids` merge. Collapsed to one slot-only query. Same door-deal columns retained so `formatPaySummary()` still gets `deal_type`/`door_pct`/`guarantee_cents`. Downstream override/enrichment logic untouched.
  2. **`preferred_artists.py:913-961 get_artist_gigs_at_venue`** (`/api/artists/{id}/gigs-at-venue/{vid}` — public venue calendar hover card). Same pattern collapsed. Kept the unfiltered-status behavior (endpoint has always returned history, not just booked) by dropping the `gs.status='booked'` filter from the slot query. Verified against Fridays Past @ venue 1: endpoint returns 15 booked rows, DB agrees, no non-booked rows exist for this pair (so no behavior change on the status axis for real data).

  **Sites deliberately left alone** (already documented above as harmless): `gigs.py:4531 list_artist_gigs` 3-leg OR (contract_hold middle leg), display-only `LEFT JOIN artists a ON g.artist_id = a.id`, payout scheduler, `update_gig` gig-level writes.

  **Phase 2e — Frontend semantic migration + more OR-EXISTS collapses + real double-count bug in venue suspension counter (July 6 same-day):**
  1. **Frontend `is_multi_slot` → `slots.length > 1` semantic (5 files)** ([artist-booked-gigs-modal.js:77](app/static/js/artist-booked-gigs-modal.js), [gig-modal.js:95](app/static/js/gig-modal.js), [gig-hover-card.js:143,167](app/static/js/gig-hover-card.js), [flyer-editor.js:2594](app/static/js/flyer-editor.js), [venue.create-gigs.js:2739-2749](app/static/js/venue.create-gigs.js)). All backend-flag reads (`data.is_multi_slot`) that gated branching are now guarded on the true multi-slot semantic — presence of 2+ slots. Every gig has `is_multi_slot=1` post-invariant, so those reads were vacuously true and treated every effectively-single-slot gig as multi-slot (adding a "Slot 1" chip, dropping artist name from the hover-card header, etc.). Fixed:
     - `artist-booked-gigs-modal.js` synthesizes `is_multi_slot: g.slots.length > 1` on emitted rows so the "Slot N" chip only renders when it means something.
     - `gig-modal.js` swap under artist-view "omit umbrella time" branch to `slots.length > 1`.
     - `gig-hover-card.js` two branch guards migrated: hydrated case → `slots.length > 1`, hydration-race case → `total_slots_count > 1`.
     - `flyer-editor.js` artist-logo picker now gated on `slots.length > 1` (redundant `is_multi_slot &&` dropped).
     - `venue.create-gigs.js` per-slot bubble no longer needs to force `is_multi_slot: false` on the hover-card payload since trimming `slots: [slot]` (length=1) automatically routes through the single-slot branch under the new gate.
  2. **`preferred_artists.py:629 / 727 / 838 / 1497` — 4 more OR-EXISTS collapses**. All four sites had the same `g.artist_id = :aid OR EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM gig_slots ...)` fallback pattern. Post-backfill the first leg is dead code. Dropped. Sites: `get_venue_artist_stats` non-preferred-artist history join, `get_venue_artist_past_gigs`, `get_venue_artist_past_gigs_summary`, `unban_venue_artist` history check.
  3. **`reviews.py:410 / 573 / 761` — 3 OR-EXISTS collapses**. Same pattern in three review-authorization checks (`submit_gig_venue_review`, `list_pending_venue_reviews`, `submit_venue_review_from_gig`). All dropped the `g.artist_id = :aid` leg; slot-lookup covers both shapes post-backfill.
  4. **`stripe_connect.py:2368 get_venue_payment_status` — REAL BUG**: the "booked gigs affected by suspension" counter was `UNION ALL` of (booked single-slot gigs at gig-level) + (booked slots at slot-level). Post-backfill every booked single-slot gig has BOTH — the union double-counted them, same failure mode as the analytics bug fixed in Phase 2b. Verified on real data: venue 1 has 20 real booked slots but the counter would have said 34 (a **70% inflation**). Collapsed to slot-only; new count = 20 = truth. Semantic: "one booked slot per affected artist payment," which is what the venue's suspension modal messaging implies.

  **Effect on real queries**:
  - Venue suspension modal now shows accurate "N bookings would be affected" — no more inflated warnings that would spook venues in an outage scenario.
  - Effectively-single-slot gigs (gigs with exactly 1 slot) render without the "Slot 1" chip in modals/CSVs/PDFs, cleaner UX.
  - Hover-card header shows artist name for effectively-single-slot bookings again — previously the multi-slot branch dropped the chip.

  **Update_gig gig-level writes STILL deferred**: `update_gig` (`gigs.py:3487`) writes `start_time / end_time / pay / artist_type / band_formats / styles` at the gig level as dupes of slot data, then re-syncs from slots at end of endpoint. Freezing the initial write would require rewriting ~30 reader sites (email templates, admin views, list endpoints) that still read gig-level fields as the umbrella. Pre-launch this is a full sprint, not a same-day cleanup. Keep as-is.

  **Payment path STILL deferred**: `_create_booking_transaction`, `book_gig`, `book_slot`, and `payout_scheduler.py` slot-first-with-gig-fallback pattern all functionally correct and money-risk-adjacent. Don't touch pre-launch.

  Import-check clean across all touched modules; API restarted clean; smoke-test: `/api/gigs/public` 200, `/api/artists/1/gigs-at-venue/1` 200; zero errors in journalctl.

  **Phase 2f — Payment-path audit + real bug in transfer-failed email (July 6 same-day, final unification pass):** User asked for a real-world payment test to be viable, which meant an end-to-end audit of the money path — every branch on `is_multi_slot` / `gigs.artist_id` in booking, cancel, countersign, payout, and email code. Verdict: the payment path is already slot-shape-correct; one email semantic bug found and fixed.
  1. **`payout_scheduler._send_transfer_failed_emails` — REAL BUG** ([payout_scheduler.py:1710-1735](backend/payout_scheduler.py)). The failed-transfer email substitutes `{{start_time}}` and `{{end_time}}` into the template. Was reading `gig_info['start_time']` / `gig_info['end_time']` — the gig UMBRELLA times (min-slot-start / max-slot-end from `update_gig`'s sync). For a multi-slot gig where the artist's actual slot was e.g. 8-10pm within a 7-11pm gig, the failure email would tell the artist "Time: 7:00 PM – 11:00 PM" — the wrong window. Now looks up the artist's booked slot in `gig_slots` first and falls back to gig-level only if no booked slot for that artist (defensive for legacy rows). Sister emails (`_send_payout_email`, `_send_venue_charged_email`) already used per-slot data via `_compute_slot_times_sqlite` — this was the laggard.
  2. **`book_gig` audit** ([gigs.py:2275-2830](backend/routes/gigs.py)). Naturally slot-shaped: finds one open slot, atomically claims via `UPDATE gig_slots SET artist_id/status WHERE status='open'`, only writes `gigs.status='booked', gigs.artist_id=:aid` when `open_count == 0` (all slots filled). Transaction created via `_create_booking_transaction(slot_id=open_slot['id'])`. No `is_multi_slot` branch. Belt-and-suspenders `gigs.artist_id` write at line 2775 kept — matches `update_gig` dupe pattern that readers still expect.
  3. **`book_slot` audit** ([gigs.py:4635-5300](backend/routes/gigs.py)). Purpose-built for slot booking, no `is_multi_slot` branch. Same atomic claim + duplicate-slot check + transaction creation shape.
  4. **`_create_booking_transaction` audit** ([gigs.py:459-820](backend/routes/gigs.py)). Function is slot-shape-agnostic. Reads deal terms from `gig_slots` when `slot_id` provided (all callers pass slot_id post-unification). Parent-child model normalized by `_recompute_gig_fees` which reads amounts from child transactions, not gig fields.
  5. **Countersign fallback in contracts.py** ([contracts.py:1832-1842](backend/routes/contracts.py)). The `if _cs_slot_rows: ... else: _create_booking_transaction(slot_id=None)` fallback is defensive dead code post-invariant (every gig has slots). Kept for safety — the else branch is unreachable in normal flow but survives as a guard against a hypothetical race where slot rows disappear between the SELECT and the transaction insert.
  6. **`payout_scheduler` defense-in-depth guard** ([payout_scheduler.py:342-367](backend/payout_scheduler.py)). Verified intact: stalled-transfer retry gate requires parent `status IN ('charged', 'paid', 'transferred')` before firing an artist_payout child transfer, preventing the "transfer money the parent never raised" incident (Changelog 2026-05-07). No changes needed.
  7. **`_recompute_gig_fees` audit** ([gigs.py:211](backend/routes/gigs.py)). Slot-shape-agnostic (reads amounts from child transactions). Guards on parent status='scheduled' prevent recompute after money has moved. No changes needed.
  8. **Cancel paths audit** (`cancel_gig`, `cancel_slot`, `delete_gig_with_slots` in [gigs.py](backend/routes/gigs.py)). All three route through the canonical `cleanup_gig_records` helper — same transaction cleanup, flyer cleanup, and `_record_artist_cancellation` (writes to `gig_cancelled_artists` for set-based exclusion). Multi-slot cancel snapshots ALL booked/pending slots and iterates per artist. The May 2026 audit fixes for this path (widened status filter, `_record_artist_cancellation` per artist, cleanup outside try/except) are all in place. No changes needed.

  **Deliberately left as-is** (functionally correct, changing risks regressions with no user-visible benefit):
  - `update_gig` gig-level dupe writes at [gigs.py:3487](backend/routes/gigs.py) — the initial UPDATE writes start_time/end_time/pay/artist_type/band_formats/styles from the venue's form data. When slots are also updated, the slot-derived sync at line 3587 overwrites start_time/end_time/pay from `gig_slots` (min-start / max-end / max-pay). For title-only edits (no slots included), the initial write is preserved. Result: `gigs.start_time/end_time/pay` are always coherent with slot data. Readers (~30 email + admin sites) still expect the umbrella fields to be non-NULL; freezing writes would require a full reader-rewrite sprint before it's safe.
  - `book_gig` gig-level `artist_id` write on all-slots-filled — vestigial belt-and-suspenders, but harmless since readers already handle NULL for multi-slot cases via slot lookup.
  - `_send_payout_email` / `_send_venue_charged_email` SELECT of `g.pay` — dead column in the SELECT, not rendered anywhere in the email body (payment amount comes from `txn.amount_cents` / `artist_payout_cents`). Removing it is cosmetic; leaves the SELECT list stable.
  - Display-only `LEFT JOIN artists a ON g.artist_id = a.id` (~8 more sites in gigs.py) — populates a display name that NULLs out for multi-slot gigs, and downstream code has always handled NULL. Safe to leave.
  - Scheduler `AND (g.artist_id IS NULL OR g.artist_id = '')` guards — belt-and-suspenders on `status='open'` in the open-gig-notification and radius-blast queries. Functionally correct, don't touch.

  **Real-world payment test viability**: end-to-end path is safe. A venue can book a gig (single or multi-slot), the transaction row is created with slot_id, `_recompute_gig_fees` normalizes across booked artists, the payout scheduler charges the venue day-of-gig, transfers to the artist day-after, and the confirmation emails render correct slot-specific times, dates, and pay strings. Cancel paths (all 3) clean up transactions via `cleanup_gig_records`, respect the `assert_no_charged_transactions` guard, and record each cancelled artist for exclusion from re-blast. Both services restarted clean; smoke-test all 5 endpoints returned 200 (or 307 for `/`); zero errors in journalctl for both `gigsfill` and `gigsfill-scheduler`. **Ready for live payment testing.**

  **Phase 2g — Full email template sweep + scheduler guard removal (July 6 same-day, TRULY final):** User pushed for 100% completion before payment testing. Grep'd every template using `{{start_time}}`/`{{end_time}}` (11 sites, 10 distinct templates) and traced each caller to check whether the substituted values came from the gig UMBRELLA (`g.start_time`/`g.end_time` — wrong for multi-slot artists) or from the artist's SLOT (`gs.start_time`/`gs.end_time` — correct). Found and fixed 3 more umbrella-time bugs, and dropped 2 belt-and-suspenders guards that were actually suppressing legitimate blasts.
  1. **`scheduler.py:379 process_gig_confirmation` — REAL BUG** ([scheduler.py:379-394](backend/scheduler.py)). Day-of gig confirmation reminder was substituting gig-umbrella `g.start_time`/`g.end_time` into a per-artist template. For a multi-slot gig where the artist's actual set was 8-10pm within a 7-11pm gig, the day-of reminder showed "Time: 7:00 PM – 11:00 PM" — the wrong window. Fixed to `gs.start_time`/`gs.end_time` from the joined `gig_slots` row (query already joined on slot for `pay` and `artist_id`, so switching times is one-line).
  2. **`email_dispatch.py:1086 send_approval_request_emails` — REAL BUG** ([email_dispatch.py:1086-1153](backend/services/email_dispatch.py)). Same-day booking approval requests to venue + pending notifications to artist substituted gig-umbrella times. Extended the existing `_slot_row` lookup (which already fetched `pay/deal_type/door_pct/guarantee_cents` for door-deal-aware pay rendering) to include `start_time/end_time`, and used them in the shared `email_vars` dict. Both the `venue_booking_approval_request` and `artist_booking_pending_approval` emails now render correct slot windows.
  3. **`email_dispatch.py:1197 send_approval_decision_emails` — REAL BUG** ([email_dispatch.py:1197-1219](backend/services/email_dispatch.py)). Approved/denied decision emails to the artist had the same pattern — gig-umbrella times substituted into a per-artist template. Same fix: extended `_slot_row` SELECT to include start/end times, wired into `email_vars`.
  4. **`scheduler.py:389,1093 open-gig blast guards — REAL BEHAVIOR BUG DROPPED** ([scheduler.py:389](backend/scheduler.py), [scheduler.py:1093](backend/scheduler.py)). Both `process_open_gig_notifications` (4w/2w/1w/36h scheduled reminders) and `process_radius_blast_for_cancelled_gig` (radius blast for cancelled gigs) had `AND (g.artist_id IS NULL OR g.artist_id = '')` as a defensive guard on top of `g.status='open'`. Verified against real data: gig 539 has status='open' (1 of 3 slots open, 2 booked) with a STALE `gigs.artist_id=3` left over from an old book_gig write path. The guard was SUPPRESSING legitimate blasts on that still-open slot 3 — preferred artists never got notified. Post-invariant, `g.status='open'` alone correctly identifies gigs with open slots (book_gig only flips gig-level status to 'booked' when the LAST slot books). Dropping the guard makes partial-multi-slot open gigs blast-eligible again.

Waitlist offers say "the gig runs 7-11pm, here are the open slots inside" with a `slots_html` block listing per-slot options. The artist hasn't picked a slot yet, so umbrella window + per-slot picker is the right shape.

  **Phase 2h — Deeper hunt, 3 more real bugs (July 6 same-day, ACTUALLY final):** User pushed back — "is waitlist still not right, check all other functions." Deep audit of every email + notification caller uncovered 3 additional umbrella-time bugs (one of which is in a legal document).
  1. **Custom contract template `{{gig_start_time}}/{{gig_end_time}}` — LEGAL DOCUMENT BUG** ([contracts.py:2662-2668](backend/routes/contracts.py)). The `custom_builder` contract path was substituting gig UMBRELLA times into a legally-binding Performance Time field. For a multi-slot gig where the artist booked slot 2 (8-10pm within a 7-11pm gig), the signed contract said "Performance Time: 7:00 PM – 11:00 PM" — a wrong contractual commitment. The `auto_generated` contract path already handled this correctly via `generate_auto_contract(slot_id=...)`; custom_builder was the laggard. Fixed to look up the slot's times from `gig_slots` when `slot_id` is provided, with gig-level fallback for the single-slot case.
  2. **Contract-hold-expired cancellation email umbrella times** ([contracts.py:3239-3252](backend/routes/contracts.py)). When Artist B's 48h contract hold on slot 2 expired, the cancellation-email dispatcher was passed `gig.start_time` / `gig.end_time` from a gig-level SELECT. On a multi-slot gig, that's the umbrella window, not B's slot. Fixed the SELECT to LEFT JOIN `gig_slots` on `(gc.artist_id, status in pending states)` and prefer the slot's times via COALESCE.
  3. **`book_gig` in-app notification empty stub** ([gigs.py:2791-2799](backend/routes/gigs.py)). The primary book_gig hot path passed `{"venue_name": "", "artist_name": "", "date": gig["date"]}` to `notify_gig_booked` — no venue name, no artist name, no time. Activity Center rendered "You booked a gig at  on DATE at " (empty venue + empty time). Book_slot's equivalent call correctly fetches the full details; book_gig was a laggard. Now runs one SELECT that pulls venue name, artist name, and the artist's slot start_time in a single query and populates the notification dict.

- **2026-07-06 (Artist daily digest — new "Nearby open gigs" section for non-preferred venues):** User feedback: the digest currently only surfaces gigs the artist can book directly (preferred at the venue, or in-radius at a venue with `blast_all_enabled`). If those are thin, the email feels empty and the artist misses local venues they could reach out to for Preferred Status. Added a second section listing nearby gigs at venues where the artist is NOT preferred yet, with a CTA to request preferred status.
  1. **New fetch mode** ([services/open_gig_digest.py:284](backend/services/open_gig_digest.py)) — `_fetch_artist_open_gigs_live` now takes `mode: str = "eligible"` (default, current behavior) or `mode="nearby_non_preferred"`. The nearby mode swaps only the eligibility gate: `if venue in preferred_venue_ids: continue` + `if distance > nearby_radius_miles (default 20): continue`. Every other filter (type / lineup / style / equipment / bans / blackouts / frequency) is shared, so we never surface a gig the artist can't legitimately book. Each returned row carries `not_preferred_at_venue=True` for the renderer.
  2. **Renderer section** ([services/open_gig_digest.py:_render_digest_email_live](backend/services/open_gig_digest.py)) — `_render_digest_email_live` now accepts `nearby_rows` + `nearby_radius_miles`. New section renders below "Open gigs you can book" with header "Nearby open gigs — you're not a Preferred Artist yet (N)" plus a small copy paragraph explaining the "Request Preferred Status" flow. Uses the same `_fmt_line` helper as the other sections so multi-slot gigs render consistently ("Slot 1 · time · pay").
  3. **Caller dedup** ([services/open_gig_digest.py:process_artist_daily_digest](backend/services/open_gig_digest.py)) — the per-user loop pulls both eligible + nearby lists per artist and dedupes nearby against BOTH `seen_nearby_ids` (across the user's artists) AND `seen_open_ids` (the "you can book" list) so a multi-artist user with one preferred + one not doesn't see the same venue's gig in both sections.
  4. **Radius default 20 mi** — hard-coded at the caller site (`_NEARBY_RADIUS_MI = 20`) with a note to make it per-artist configurable if we want to expose the setting. `_fetch_artist_open_gigs_live(mode="nearby_non_preferred")` takes it as a parameter so future callers can override.

  **Verified end-to-end**: preview render for Fridays Past showed both sections — "Open gigs you can book (1)" for the 14 Cannons Jul 17 gig where they're preferred, plus "Nearby open gigs — you're not a Preferred Artist yet (1)" for a test gig at V8 (Venue Test, 0.0 mi away) where they aren't. Dedup verified against the eligible set. Sections separated with the same visual spacing pattern as booked vs open. Both services restarted clean; no errors in logs.

- **2026-07-04 (1099-K integration — W-9 pre-fill on Stripe Connect create + Tax Center link + venue TPSO-exception callout):** Fully wired 1099-K generation so at year-end, artists get their tax forms from Stripe automatically and venues know they're off the hook for 1099-NEC.
  1. **Discovery — Express blocks post-create individual.\* updates.** Initial attempt was to sync W-9 → `stripe.Account.modify(individual=...)` on every W-9 save so Stripe always had the current TIN. Verified empirically: Stripe Express returns `"This application does not have the required permissions for the parameter 'individual'"` for first_name/last_name/address AND id_number — the platform can only pass `individual` at `Account.create` time; after that, the artist owns edits via the Stripe Express dashboard directly. Refactored accordingly.
  2. **W-9 pre-fill at Account.create** ([routes/tax.py:build_stripe_individual_from_w9](backend/routes/tax.py)) — new helper reads the artist's saved W-9 (`w9_forms` table), decrypts the TIN via existing Fernet path, parses `tax_name` into first/last, and returns a Stripe `individual` dict with name + `id_number` + address. [routes/stripe_connect.py:340](backend/routes/stripe_connect.py) `Account.create` now calls this and threads the result into the `individual=` param when non-None. Effect: if the artist filed a W-9 before clicking "Connect to Stripe" (common because venues can require W-9 upfront), Stripe onboarding starts already populated instead of asking for name/SSN/address again. Non-blocking — Account.create still fires if pre-fill assembly errors. The obsolete `sync_w9_to_stripe_connect` name is kept as a no-op that returns `"express_locks_individual_updates"` so any stale callers don't crash.
  3. **1099-K & Tax Documents UI card** ([artist-book-gigs.html:2011-2039](app/artist-book-gigs.html)) — new content-card in the artist Legal → Tax Information subtab, sits right under the W-9 form. Explains that Stripe issues the 1099-K (not the venue via 1099-NEC), cites current IRS thresholds ($2,500 for TY 2026, $600 for TY 2027+), points to the W-9 as the source of pre-fill data, and has an "🧾 Open Stripe Tax Center" button that hits the existing `POST /api/stripe/artist/{id}/dashboard-link` endpoint (login-link via `stripe.Account.create_login_link`) and opens the Stripe Express dashboard in a new tab. Button handler at [artist-book-gigs-init.js:openStripeTaxCenter](app/static/js/artist-book-gigs-init.js) surfaces friendly errors when the artist hasn't onboarded yet ("head to Payments tab and click Connect to Stripe") vs generic Stripe failures.
  4. **Venue-facing TPSO-exception callout** ([venue-create-gigs.html:2807-2814](app/venue-create-gigs.html)) — green banner at the top of the Payments tab explains that because GigsFill routes payments through Stripe (a Third-Party Settlement Organization), venues **don't need to file 1099-NEC** for artists paid via the platform — Stripe files the 1099-K directly. Includes a footnote that cash paid outside GigsFill (walk-in gigs, tips) is still their responsibility to report. Big sales/demo talking point: "one less thing at year-end."
  5. **Smoke-tested against a real W-9**: `build_stripe_individual_from_w9(artist=1)` returns `{first_name: 'John', last_name: 'Carta', id_number: <9-digit>, address: {line1, city, state, postal_code, country: 'US'}}` — verified. Express platform-permission errors on post-create modify verified. No-W-9 branch returns `None` cleanly. `create_login_link` endpoint verified pre-existing.

  **Follow-ups deferred**: (a) Verify Stripe Connect dashboard has 1099-K auto-issue toggled ON for the platform account — that's a dashboard-only setting (Connect → Settings → Tax Reporting), not something the code can flip. (b) Business W-9 filings (LLC/S-Corp) currently get treated as individuals since Express-US is individual-only; long term the platform could split into Standard/Custom accounts for entity payees. (c) 1099-K info could also surface in the artist's earnings emails around Jan 31 as a "your 1099-K is ready" nudge.

  **Same-day follow-up — onboarding checklist reordered so pre-fill actually helps** ([routes/onboarding.py:79-99](backend/routes/onboarding.py)) — `ARTIST_TASKS` used to list Payments (Stripe Connect) before Tax Information (W-9). That meant new artists set up Stripe onboarding FIRST — before their W-9 existed — so the just-shipped pre-fill helper `build_stripe_individual_from_w9` never had data to pass. Swapped: Tax Information is now step 1 with an explicit `"(do this first)"` cue in the title and description that explains the pre-fill benefit ("we'll pre-fill your Stripe onboarding with your legal name, address, and TIN so you don't type them twice"). Payments (step 2) mirrors the pointer ("if you completed the W-9 above first, Stripe onboarding starts already filled in"). Checklist UI ([static/js/onboarding-checklist.js](app/static/js/onboarding-checklist.js)) renders in whatever order the API returns, so the swap propagated automatically — no frontend change. Venue tasks left alone since VENUE_TASKS.tax_settings is a policy toggle ("do you require W-9 from artists?") not a pre-fill source.

  **Cache bumped** on `artist-book-gigs-init.js?v=11`. API restarted; sync smoke passed; no error logs.

- **2026-07-03 (Email Notifications tab consolidation — hard-wired intervals + per-window waive_frequency + cancellation blasts folded into open-gig rows):** The Email Notifications tab (Venue → Email Center → Email Notifications) grew organically and had four semi-overlapping sections doing similar jobs. Consolidated everything into the four Open Gig rows (4w / 2w / 1w / 36h), each of which now owns its scheduled reminder AND its cancellation blast. Also polished several downstream surfaces surfaced by the same review.
  1. **Intervals hard-wired** ([scheduler.py:335,555,1035](backend/scheduler.py), [routes/gigs.py:6377](backend/routes/gigs.py)) — every scheduled-reminder cadence is now a constant, not venue-configurable. `gig_confirmation`=1 week before, `open_gig_4w/2w/1w/36h`=matched to key name, `cancelled_blast` legacy window=7 days, `radius_blast`=36h. `venue_email_notifications.time_value` + `time_unit` columns stay in the schema for backwards compat but no code reads them. UI replaced the number+unit inputs with static labels ("Fires 4 weeks before gig date") and hidden `<input type="hidden">` shims so the existing save loop stays harmless.
  2. **Per-open-gig `waive_frequency` toggle** ([db.py:1153](backend/db.py), [scheduler.py:582](backend/scheduler.py)) — new `venue_email_notifications.waive_frequency INTEGER DEFAULT 1` column. Previously every 4w/2w/1w/36h reminder unconditionally set `gigs.frequency_exempt=1` (so any preferred artist could book regardless of their frequency limit). Now the venue chooses per row. `process_open_gig_notifications` only fires the UPDATE when `waive_frequency=1`. Scheduler uses per-key `COALESCE(waive_frequency, ?)` fallback: 4w/2w default OFF, 1w/36h default ON — matches the venue-user spec.
  3. **Cancellation blast folded into open-gig rows** ([db.py:1161-1163](backend/db.py), [routes/gigs.py:6377-6472](backend/routes/gigs.py)) — added `cancel_notify_preferred`, `cancel_notify_all_enabled`, `cancel_notify_all_radius` columns. `fire_cancelled_gig_blast` rewritten with a **smallest-covering-window rule**: on cancellation, walk 36h → 1w → 2w → 4w and pick the smallest window where (a) `days_until_gig ≤ window` AND (b) `cancel_notify_preferred=1 OR cancel_notify_all_enabled=1`. That row's settings drive the send; each cancellation fires exactly one blast. `waive_frequency` on the same row is honored (gates the `frequency_exempt=1` UPDATE). Old `cancelled_blast` row is no longer consulted — the CANCELLED GIG BLAST + RADIUS BLAST sections in the UI are wrapped in `display:none` so the JS save/load stays harmless and legacy rows remain dormant.
  4. **New-venue defaults** ([routes/venue_emails.py:382](backend/routes/venue_emails.py)) — `NOTIFICATION_DEFAULTS` extended with per-key `waive_frequency` + `cancel_notify_*`. Brand-new venue with no rows in `venue_email_notifications` gets:

     | Row | Enabled | Waive Freq | Blast ALL | Cancel→Preferred | Cancel→ALL |
     |---|:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|
     | 4 Week | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |
     | 2 Week | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |
     | 1 Week | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ | ❌ |
     | 36 Hours | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ |

     The scheduler's `_DEFAULT_CANCEL` map in `fire_cancelled_gig_blast` mirrors these exactly so a venue that never opened the settings page still gets the intended cancellation behavior.
  5. **UI polish** ([venue-create-gigs.html:2475-2604](app/venue-create-gigs.html)) — every setting row's Blink label right-aligned via `margin-left:auto`. Every open-gig row now shows 4 sub-toggles: Waive Frequency, Blast ALL within X miles, GIG CANCELLED → Preferred, GIG CANCELLED → ALL other within X miles. Each descriptive `<span>` has a hover `title="..."` explaining what checking/unchecking actually does. Row descriptions rewritten from "Email all Preferred Artists…" to plainer "Reminder that a gig is still open — sent X before gig date." Section header rewritten to "Email Artists about open or cancelled gigs and lift frequency limitations if desired."
  6. **Payout email — settled door share in parens** ([payout_scheduler.py:1381-1418](backend/payout_scheduler.py)) — for door-split slots, the payout email's "Gig Pay" line previously showed `$10.00 guarantee + 50% of door` with no indication of what the actual door share turned out to be. Extended `_door_row` query to also SELECT `door_receipts_cents` + `settled_at`, and when the slot is settled, the actual share is appended in parens: `$10.00 guarantee + 50% of door ($2.50)`. Matches the format the venue-side website already uses ([api-globals.js:224](app/static/js/api-globals.js)). Flat slots and unsettled door slots render unchanged.
  7. **Venue Payments query — surface multi-slot gigs with any booked slot** ([stripe_connect.py:2000-2011](backend/routes/stripe_connect.py)) — multi-slot gigs stay `g.status='open'` until every slot is booked, so a real venue_charge with booked slots on an otherwise-open gig was invisible on the Payments tab (venue reported not seeing gig 539's 7/1 charge). Added an `EXISTS` clause: also surface any gig where at least one slot is in a bookable state (`booked`/`pending_contract`/`awaiting_venue_contract`/`pending_venue_approval`/`started`/`completed`/`paid`).
  8. **Digest email — always render `Slot 1 · time · pay`** ([open_gig_digest.py:848](backend/services/open_gig_digest.py)) — the daily open-gigs digest rendered single-slot gigs inline (`date · venue · time · pay`) but multi-slot gigs with header + Slot N sub-lines. User asked for consistency. Removed the `if len(slots) <= 1` tight-line branch; every gig now renders as header + Slot lines, with a synthesized Slot 1 for legacy gigs with no `gig_slots` row.
  9. **Schema + ORM** — [db.py:1153-1163](backend/db.py) adds four new columns via `_add_columns` (idempotent); [models.py:451-458](backend/models.py) synced. Save/load endpoint in [routes/venue_emails.py:392-528](backend/routes/venue_emails.py) round-trips every new field with `COALESCE` fallbacks. JS `_NOTIF_ID_MAP` in [venue-create-gigs-init.js:261-330](app/static/js/venue-create-gigs-init.js) extended for the 4 open-gig keys; save + load loops both read/write the new fields.

  **Gig 539 verified end-to-end**: fired at 00:00 UTC 2026-07-03 (5 PM PT 2026-07-02). Parent 339 `charged` with `pi_3TouWzGTPqz6PmNX0LOePvKO`. Child 340 (Fridays Past) `transferred` with `tr_3TouWzGTPqz6PmNX06qd6dJG`, $7.78 net. Child 341 (Fifty Proof, settled door $10 guarantee + $2.50 share = $12.50 basis) `transferred` with `tr_3TouWzGTPqz6PmNX08Ii1OMF`, $9.72 net. Override was NOT applied ([gig_hold.py:549](backend/services/gig_hold.py) rule verified — `apply_override=0` on slot).

  **Cache bumped** on `venue-create-gigs-init.js?v=10` and `artist.book-gigs.js?v=173`. Both `gigsfill` + `gigsfill-scheduler` services restarted; four new DB columns verified present.

  **Post-launch adversarial audit — 2 launch-blockers caught + fixed same day:**
  - **BUG #1 (critical) — `fire_cancelled_gig_blast` crashed on every real fire.** [routes/gigs.py:6653](backend/routes/gigs.py) preferred-artists SELECT had `:excl_aid` mentioned in a `--` SQL comment. SQLAlchemy `text()` scans the entire query body for `:name` patterns and turns each into a positional `?` bind, **including inside comments**. Token stripping happens later so SQLite saw 8 `?` positions but SQLAlchemy handed 9 bindings → `sqlite3.ProgrammingError: Incorrect number of bindings supplied. The current statement uses 8, and there are 9 supplied`. Every cancellation stamped the calendar token and set `frequency_exempt` but zero preferred emails went out. Fix: rewrite the comment to avoid the colon-plus-identifier pattern. Verified against the live query at production venue 1 — 0 rows (correct, no matching preferred artists for gig 539), no exception. Landmine: any `:name` inside a `text()` block — even quoted, even in a comment — becomes a bindparam.
  - **BUG #2 (high) — cancellation radius blast still read the legacy `radius_blast` row.** [routes/gigs.py:6843](backend/routes/gigs.py) after the preferred-artist loop, the "blast non-preferred within radius" branch queried `notification_key='radius_blast'` for its enable + miles instead of using `blast_all_en` / `blast_all_mi` from the owning open-gig window (already computed above). Consequences: (a) a venue toggling `cancel_notify_all_enabled=True` on `open_gig_36h` wouldn't fire if a legacy `radius_blast.enabled=False` row existed; (b) `cancel_notify_all_radius=50` was ignored in favor of legacy `radius_miles=20`; (c) worst — a brand-new venue with NO `radius_blast` row had `radius_notif is None`, the guard fell through, and the radius blast fired on EVERY covered cancellation regardless of the per-window intent. Fix: delete the legacy read; gate on `blast_all_en` and use `blast_all_mi` directly.
  - **BUG #3 (medium) — irrelevant per-key fields hydrating on the wire.** [routes/venue_emails.py:415-483](backend/routes/venue_emails.py) API GET returned `waive_frequency=True` + `cancel_notify_*` on every key including `gig_confirmation`/`cancelled_blast`/`radius_blast` (which don't consume them), and the frontend echoed those fields back on save, writing meaningless state into those DB rows on every visit. Fix: refactored response construction into a `_base_settings(key, defaults, ...)` helper that only includes the Jul 2026 fields when `key in _OPEN_GIG_KEYS`. Same discipline enforced on the save side — a `if key not in _OPEN_GIG_KEYS` guard forces `waive_frequency=1` + `cancel_notify_*=default` before INSERT so a stale payload can't corrupt non-open-gig rows.
  - **BUG #4 (low) — blink-color divergence for pristine vs saved-null rows.** Same file had a `_default_blink_colors` dict that disagreed with `NOTIFICATION_DEFAULTS['blink_color']` for `open_gig_1w` (amber vs green). Depending on whether a venue's row existed with NULL blink_color, they'd get one color; with no row at all, the other. Fix: deleted `_default_blink_colors` entirely — `_base_settings` now falls back to `defaults.get('blink_color', '#f59e0b')` in both the pristine and saved-null paths, so there's exactly one source of truth per key.
  - **Fallback INSERT hardening** — save endpoint's exception-catch previously dropped ALL Jul 2026 fields on any column-missing error. Now tiered: try full → try trimming Jul 2026 fields only → last-resort minimal, so schemas partway through migration keep as much of the payload as they support.
  - **Verified post-fix**: per-key GET response omits `waive_frequency`/`cancel_notify_*` for the 3 non-open-gig keys (`gig_confirmation`, `cancelled_blast`, `radius_blast`) and includes them for the 4 open-gig keys. Blink-color fallbacks now match `NOTIFICATION_DEFAULTS` for every key. Save-side per-key discipline confirmed by inspection.

- **2026-07-01 (Two new artist types + equipment-gate: Open Mic MC / Karaoke MC):** Added two new artist types plus a new axis on matching. When a venue creates a slot for either MC type, a "requires artist to bring all equipment" checkbox appears (default checked). If checked, only artists whose profile says `has_own_equipment=1` can be matched with that slot. Filter applies to every downstream email/blast/booking path via the shared `_artist_matches_slot` helper.
  1. **New DB columns** ([db.py:447 + 664](backend/db.py)) — `artists.has_own_equipment BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE` and `gig_slots.requires_equipment BOOLEAN DEFAULT NULL`. Added via `_add_columns` (additive, no migration). NULL on the slot = venue didn't specify → no filter. ORM synced in `backend/models.py`.
  2. **Match helper extended** ([services/gig_hold.py:1007](backend/services/gig_hold.py)) — `_artist_matches_slot` gained `_MC_TYPES` skip-list (MC types have no band_formats / styles axis per this ticket) and an equipment gate at the end. Every downstream email/blast/booking path routes through this — automatic ripple, no per-caller change beyond fetching `has_own_equipment` alongside the existing artist fields.
  3. **Digest + 4 blast paths** — `_fetch_artist_open_gigs_live` in [open_gig_digest.py:492](backend/services/open_gig_digest.py), `process_open_gig_notifications` preferred + blast_all in [scheduler.py:704 + 887](backend/scheduler.py), `process_radius_blast` in same file, `fire_cancelled_gig_blast` in [routes/gigs.py:6634](backend/routes/gigs.py). Each: added `has_own_equipment` to the artist SELECT, added `requires_equipment` to the open-slot fetch, extended the closure to skip band_formats/styles for MC types and to fail the match when a slot requires equipment the artist hasn't opted in to.
  4. **Backend route wiring** — `POST /artists` + `PUT /artists/{id}` + `GET /artists/{id}` now accept/persist/return `has_own_equipment`. `create_gig` and 2 other slot-INSERT sites in `routes/gigs.py` now persist `requires_equipment`. Signup at [auth.py:684](backend/routes/auth.py) reads `data.get("has_own_equipment")` when creating the artist profile.
  5. **Frontend dropdowns** — 4 sites updated: [signup-new.html:305](app/signup-new.html), [user-profile.html:900](app/user-profile.html), [static/js/artist.edit.js:460](app/static/js/artist.edit.js), [static/js/venue.create-gigs.js:1070](app/static/js/venue.create-gigs.js). Added `<option value="Open Mic MC">` and `<option value="Karaoke MC">` (with emoji prefix on the venue slot picker).
  6. **Icon maps** — 21 sites across 12 files updated to include `'Open Mic MC':'🎙️'` (studio mic — distinct from Comedian's 🎤 stage mic) and `'Karaoke MC':'🎶'` (musical notes — signals "audience-driven"). Files: venue-profile.html, artist-profile.html, and 10 JS files across calendar bubbles + hover cards + hold-mgmt.
  7. **Venue "requires equipment" checkbox** ([venue.create-gigs.js:1082](app/static/js/venue.create-gigs.js)) — appears in the slot row when `slot-artist-type` is Open Mic MC or Karaoke MC. Default checked on first reveal. Type-change handler toggles visibility and marks the CB as touched so a later type switch doesn't reset a user's choice. Persists as `slot.requires_equipment` in the create-gig / update-gig payload. Threaded through 3 existing-slot `addSlotRow` callers so editing a gig preserves the value.
  8. **Artist "I have equipment" checkbox** ([artist-edit.html:665](app/artist-edit.html) + [artist.edit.js:508](app/static/js/artist.edit.js)) — new "mcEquipmentBlock" in Section 3 of artist-edit page. Hidden by default; artist-type change handler reveals it when Open Mic MC / Karaoke MC. Auto-saves via `apiPutSafe` on toggle (matches the type/formats save pattern).
  9. **Signup form** — new `#mcEquipmentField` in [signup-new.html:365](app/signup-new.html) with a `#signupHasOwnEquipment` checkbox. `toggleBandFormats()` in [signup-new-init.js:126](app/static/js/signup-new-init.js) also toggles the MC block visibility. Payload writer reads and forwards `has_own_equipment` when the artist_type is MC.

  **E2E verified against live DB**:
  - Karaoke MC artist WITHOUT equipment: does NOT see a Karaoke MC slot with `requires_equipment=1` ✓
  - Same artist WITH `has_own_equipment=1`: sees the slot ✓
  - Slot with `requires_equipment=NULL`: no filter fires — artist sees the slot regardless of their equipment field ✓
  - `_artist_matches_slot(req=1, no_equip)` → False; `(req=1, has_equip)` → True; `(req=NULL, no_equip)` → True ✓
  - MC types skip band_formats/styles filter: an Open Mic MC artist with no band_formats set sees an MC slot with band_formats='Solo' (correctly ignored) ✓
  - Test Artist DB state restored to Live Band / Solo,Duo,Trio,Full Band pre-signoff. No production data change.

  **Both services restarted clean**, cache bumped on `venue.create-gigs.js?v=221`, `artist.edit.js?v=16`, `signup-new-init.js?v=8`, `artist.book-gigs.js?v=169`.

- **2026-07-01 (Digest + blast type-match: reject mismatched-type OPEN slots on multi-slot gigs):** User reported Test Artist (Live Band) received a digest email listing gig 539's Wed 7/1 11pm–1am $200 slot. That slot is the **open Comedian slot** on a mixed multi-slot gig whose other two slots (Live Band, booked) satisfied the gig-level type filter. The filter was `gig-level type match OR any open-slot type match` — but the OR let ANY multi-slot gig with a matching gig-level type through, even when every OPEN slot was for a different type. Correct semantics: the artist must be able to actually BOOK at least one open slot. Fixes at 4 sites:
  1. **`_fetch_artist_open_gigs_live`** ([services/open_gig_digest.py:492](backend/services/open_gig_digest.py)) — the daily-digest fetcher. Now computes effective slot type per open slot (`slot.artist_type OR gig.artist_type` for legacy NULL-inherit rows) and requires at least one open slot's effective type to match. Also filters `out_slots` at render time to hide mismatched-type open slots (a Live Band artist looking at a multi-slot LB+Comedian gig only sees LB open slots).
  2. **`process_open_gig_notifications` preferred branch** ([scheduler.py:704](backend/scheduler.py)) — the SQL preferred-artist query. Rewrote the WHERE clause: Case A requires an OPEN slot whose type matches (with gig-level fallback for NULL-inherit slots), Case B falls back to gig-level type match only when the gig has zero open slot rows (legacy path).
  3. **`process_open_gig_notifications` blast_all branch** ([scheduler.py:840](backend/scheduler.py)) — the accepted-types set. When any open slot has an explicit `artist_type`, only those types are accepted (gig-level is ignored — it may match a BOOKED slot but that slot isn't bookable). Gig-level is added back to the set only if some open slot has a NULL/empty `artist_type` and inherits from gig level.
  4. **`process_radius_blast`** ([scheduler.py:1049](backend/scheduler.py)) and **`fire_cancelled_gig_blast`** ([routes/gigs.py:6634](backend/routes/gigs.py)) — same accepted-types set pattern applied.

  **E2E verified**: Test Artist (Live Band, id=4) — gig 539 correctly EXCLUDED from digest results after fix. Comedian artist WOULD see gig 539's slot 3. Other Live Band gigs (521, 540, 522, 523) unchanged. `open_slot_count` and per-slot renders remain accurate. Both api services restarted clean.

- **2026-07-01 (Overnight sweep — Postgres-ready + prod-resilience + regression polish):** After Test Mode removal, three parallel deep-dive audits (regression / Postgres readiness / production resilience) surfaced additional issues. Landed everything overnight so we come back to a full-clean bill.

  **Prod-resilience REDs (all fixed):**
  1. **Port 8001 was publicly reachable** — `curl http://gigsfill.com:8001/…` returned 200 straight from uvicorn, bypassing nginx + all security headers + HSTS + CSP + slowapi rate-limits (attacker could spoof X-Forwarded-For). Fixed by changing `ExecStart` in `/etc/systemd/system/gigsfill.service` from `--host 0.0.0.0` to `--host 127.0.0.1`. Verified externally-blocked; HTTPS via nginx still works.
  2. **Stripe SDK had no timeout / retry config** — SDK default is 80s per request with 0 retries. A Stripe outage during onboarding/checkout would hang uvicorn workers for 80s each; with 2 workers, 4 concurrent hangs = total site outage. Added `stripe.default_http_client = RequestsClient(timeout=15); stripe.max_network_retries = 2` at [main.py:233](backend/main.py) inside a try/except so it degrades gracefully if the SDK layout changes. Uses `stripe._http_client` (SDK 14.x path).
  3. **9 SessionLocal leaks in `routes/media.py`** — `db = SessionLocal()` paired with `db.close()` on the happy path only; every raise (auth 403, validation 400, ORM error) orphaned a session until the SQLAlchemy pool ran dry. Converted all 8 endpoints to `db=Depends(get_db)` which handles teardown via `get_db`'s `try/finally`.

  **Regression YELLOWs found + fixed:**
  4. **XSS on `_hold_redirect_to_contract_landing`** — the "sign your contract" email-link landing interpolated `venue_name` + `gig_date` raw into HTML. Same attacker class the Jul 1 XSS batch closed. Wrapped in `_h_esc` at [routes/gigs.py:7881](backend/routes/gigs.py).
  5. **`_record_failed_login` cross-worker race** — the DB-backed lockout still used a Python `threading.Lock` + read-then-write pattern, so two uvicorn workers could each read count=4 and both UPDATE to 5 (effective threshold doubled). Rewrote as a single atomic `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT(email, ip) DO UPDATE SET attempt_count = login_attempts.attempt_count + 1, locked_until = CASE ... END`. Verified 3 sequential inserts land count=3, and crossing the threshold stamps `locked_until` exactly once. Works on SQLite 3.24+ AND Postgres.
  6. **`_backstop_open_slot in dir()`** — fragile idiom that a nested-helper refactor would silently break. Replaced with `locals().get("_backstop_open_slot")`.
  7. **Residual `'test'` in read-side status tuples** — 4 sites in `door_settle.py`, `gigs.py`, `stripe_connect.py` had `("scheduled", "test")` gating; simplified. Two `if True:` dead conditionals in `stripe_connect.py` collapsed and inner code de-indented. Also stripped stale `'scheduled'/'test'` doc comments.
  8. **Tuple-comma sql-syntax bug** — batch-sed on `('scheduled', 'test')` had left invalid SQL `IN ('scheduled',)` at 9 sites (admin/door_settle/stripe_connect). Corrected to `= 'scheduled'`. Caught by the free-trial e2e verification.

  **Postgres-readiness REDs (all fixed):**
  9. **`_sqlite_to_pg_funcs_only` translator drift** — the ORM `text()` translator was missing the 3 new rewriters added to `_sqlite_to_pg` last night, plus `julianday('now')`. `preferred_artists.py` ORM callsites would have reached PG unrewritten. Synced both translators and extended with the same new patterns.
  10. **`GROUP_CONCAT` unrewritten** — 4 callsites (`admin.py` × 3, `stripe_connect.py`, `analytics.py`) would 500 on PG. Added a translator rewriter: `GROUP_CONCAT(x)` → `STRING_AGG((x)::text, ',')`, `GROUP_CONCAT(x, 'sep')` → `STRING_AGG((x)::text, 'sep')`. Applied in both translator variants.
  11. **`INSTR(a, b)` unrewritten** — used at `stripe_connect.py:1978-1979`. Added translator: `INSTR(a, b)` → `POSITION(b IN a)` (arg-order flipped correctly).
  12. **Parameterized `datetime('now', ?)`** — SQLite-only, regex-untranslatable. At `open_gig_digest.py:1327` (queue pruner). Fixed inline by computing the cutoff in Python and passing a plain timestamp — works on both engines.
  13. **`BOOLEAN DEFAULT 0/1` in 15 CREATE TABLE columns** — Postgres rejects (`column has type boolean but expression is of type integer`). Batch-swapped to `BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE/TRUE`. SQLite accepts both since 3.23 so no change to current prod behavior.
  14. **`sqlite_sequence` seed for support_tickets** — SQLite-only table. Branched on `_IS_POSTGRES`: PG uses `setval(pg_get_serial_sequence(…), 99999, true)`.
  15. **Flyer `executescript` + `PRAGMA foreign_keys=OFF` migration** — SQLite-only. Branched: PG uses idempotent `ALTER TABLE flyers ALTER COLUMN venue_id DROP NOT NULL`; SQLite path unchanged.
  16. **`COLLATE NOCASE` on `affiliate_recommend_emails.recipient_email`** — SQLite-only. Wrapped in `_IS_POSTGRES` check; drops the clause on PG.
  17. **11 raw `sqlite3.connect(str(DB_PATH))` calls** — every one 500'd on PG. Swept `scheduler.py:27`, `main.py:552` (healthz), `services/gig_hold.py:1606` (SMTP settings fetch), `services/system_alerts.py:92`, `services/connect_health.py:498/591/737`, `routes/admin.py:2971/3053`, `routes/emails.py:357`. All swapped to `backend.db.get_db_connection()` which returns `_PgCompatConn` on PG (with `?`→`%s` translation + `RealDictCursor`) and raw sqlite3+Row on SQLite. The two remaining `sqlite3.connect` sites are inside `db.py`'s own SQLite branches — intentional.

  **Smoke-tested post-restart:**
  - `/`, `/api/gigs/public`, `/app/public-gigs.html`, `/app/artist-book-gigs.html`, `/app/venue-create-gigs.html`, `/app/admin.html` all 200.
  - `/hold/decline/nosuchtoken` GET→404, POST→200; series variant GET→404, POST→410 (correct behavior).
  - `/api/admin/db/tables` returns 401 (auth-required, not 500 — the new `_list_tables` helper works).
  - External port 8001 refused (verified via `/dev/tcp` from public IP).
  - Zero ERROR/WARNING log lines in the 20 min since restart.
  - Scheduler running clean — Connect health audit + blast processing + Stripe API calls all succeeding.
  - `login_attempts` upsert semantics verified: 3 sequential inserts → count=3 (unlocked); crossing threshold → `locked_until` stamped once.
  - Gig 539 payment path unchanged: parent `$25 charge → $7.50/$7.50 payouts → $10 commission`, scheduled 7/3.

- **2026-07-01 (Test Mode fully removed — payments-always-live simplification):** Test Mode existed as scaffolding to let the entire booking → charge → transfer → settle pipeline exercise end-to-end without hitting Stripe. Now that the platform is going full production, every branch that dispatched on `payments_enabled` / `is_test` / `status='test'` / `stripe_transfer_id='test_transfer'` collapses to one live path. Cleanup:
  1. **Booking always writes `status='scheduled'`** — [routes/gigs.py:567](backend/routes/gigs.py). `_create_booking_transaction` no longer reads `payments_enabled`; every child+parent transaction is inserted at `scheduled` regardless. Dead code path (`tx_status = 'scheduled' if payments_live else 'test'`) removed.
  2. **Payout scheduler simplified** — [payout_scheduler.py:114](backend/payout_scheduler.py). Removed `payments_live` read, `is_test = txn["status"] == "test"` variable, and the entire `if is_test:` branch (~55 lines) that flipped rows to `transferred/test_transfer` + `_send_payout_email` + affiliate-accrual-skip. The `WHERE t.status IN ('scheduled', 'test', 'charge_retry')` gate is now `('scheduled', 'charge_retry')`. Three `and payments_live` clauses on the retry/stale-transfer sweeps dropped. `_settle_test_transactions()` (100-line function that ran every minute to auto-flip test transfers to `paid` after 2 h) deleted; the scheduler loop no longer calls it — every minute now runs one fewer poll.
  3. **Stripe Connect gates hard-block everywhere** — [routes/gigs.py:2263,2599,4939](backend/routes/gigs.py), [services/gig_hold.py:475,2306](backend/services/gig_hold.py). Every booking path used to have `if str(_pay_on).lower() in ('1','true'):` softening the Connect onboarding gate — an artist without Connect could book during "test mode." Now every path hard-blocks on missing Connect onboarding: `book_gig`, `book_slot`, `respond_to_hold_offer`, `_book_one_series_pick`, and the pre-book checks that `book_with_contract` runs.
  4. **`stripe_connect.py` fee-charge / cancel / reinstate paths always live** — [stripe_connect.py:830,1149,1614,1670,1691](backend/routes/stripe_connect.py). Removed 6 `if payments_live:` gates around real Stripe API calls. `xfer_id: transfer_id or ('test_transfer' if not payments_live else None)` reduced to `xfer_id: transfer_id`. `new_status` derivation for reinstatement simplified from a 3-branch `if/elif/else` to `'transferred' if transfer_id else 'charged'`. `get_stripe_keys(db)` no longer reads `payments_enabled` and pins the returned value to `True` for any legacy consumer.
  5. **Admin free-trial-restore always restores to `scheduled`** — [routes/admin.py:1373](backend/routes/admin.py). The `restore_status = 'scheduled' if payments_live else 'test'` conditional simplified to unconditional `'scheduled'`.
  6. **Affiliate 1099 threshold check drops `payments_enabled`** — [routes/affiliate.py:1078](backend/routes/affiliate.py). `has_stripe` now depends purely on Stripe key presence.
  7. **`status IN ('scheduled', 'test')` tuples cleaned** across `door_settle.py`, `stripe_connect.py`, `admin.py`, `me.py`. Every WRITE / re-settle / cancel-money-in-flight gate now checks only `'scheduled'` (+ `'charge_retry'` where the retry branch applies). READ queries that list every historical status (e.g. earnings history at stripe_connect.py:2178) still include `'test'` — legacy rows keep rendering correctly for the ~14 already-paid historical test transactions.
  8. **Admin UI: "Payments ON/OFF" toggle removed** — [app/admin.html:825](app/admin.html) collapsed to a static "💳 Payments · LIVE" badge. [admin-platform.js:597-663](app/static/js/admin-platform.js) `togglePaymentsEnabled` + `setToggle` reduced to no-ops (kept as callable stubs so cached DOM handlers don't throw). `loadPaymentSettings` / `autoSavePaymentSettings` / `loadSiteStatusToggles` no longer send or read `payments_enabled`. Cache bumped to `admin-platform.js?v=8`.
  9. **Platform setting `payments_enabled` deprecated in place** — [db.py:1421](backend/db.py). Default seed changed from `'0'` to `'1'` with a description marking it deprecated. The row is kept for any external tooling that reads it; nothing in-tree does. Current production row flipped to `'1'`.
  10. **No data migration needed**. Pre-flip counts: 0 rows in `status='test'`, 0 rows with `stripe_transfer_id='test_transfer'`. All historical test-mode rows had already terminated at `paid` (14 rows) via the auto-settle path. Gig 539 tomorrow's real-money charge unchanged: `$25 charge → $7.50/$7.50 payouts → $10 commission`, scheduled 7/3.

  **Restart + smoke-tested**: both services `active`, `/api/gigs/public` 200, no errors in log. Every parse-check clean. Net: ~200 lines of branching code removed, `_settle_test_transactions` deleted, minute-poll gone.

- **2026-07-01 (Pre-launch full audit — 9 RED + high-value YELLOW fix pass):** Ran a 5-agent parallel deep-audit spanning payments, booking/cancel, holds/schedulers/email, auth/admin/DB, and frontend security + production-readiness. Cross-agent consolidation: 9 REDs + 36 YELLOWs. Landed every RED plus the load-bearing YELLOWs in one commit-sized pass. Nothing pre-launch-blocking remains.
  1. **XSS — venue.create-gigs.js / artist.book-gigs.js / admin-platform.js / signup-new-init.js**. Multiple `${gig.artist_name}`, `${g.venue_name}`, `${v.notes}`, `${entry.artist_name}` interpolations in `innerHTML`-bound template strings went un-escaped. Attacker-controlled display names (venues + artists set their own names) executed script in the opposite side's session. Every offending site now wraps the value in `esc()` (already imported globally via `security.js`). Sites: [venue.create-gigs.js:3272, 3288, 4302, 5858, 6259](app/static/js/venue.create-gigs.js), [artist.book-gigs.js:733-738](app/static/js/artist.book-gigs.js), [admin-platform.js:758-763](app/static/js/admin-platform.js), [signup-new-init.js:293](app/static/js/signup-new-init.js).
  2. **`/hold/decline/{token}` prefetcher defense** — [routes/gigs.py:7910](backend/routes/gigs.py). Same shape as the Jun 30 Y3 series-hold fix. New `_resolve_hold_token` helper looks up artist / venue / date without mutation; auth'd caller who owns the artist decodes immediately (one-click as before); un-auth'd caller sees a confirmation form and must POST to the sibling `hold_decline_submit` endpoint. Gmail/Outlook/Slack link prefetchers can no longer silently fire the decline.
  3. **`/api/gigs/{id}/approve-booking` prefetcher defense** — [routes/gigs.py:5182](backend/routes/gigs.py). `approve_booking` is bound to both GET and POST. The GET path with a token was auto-executing approval (and auto-creating a signed contract row!) — a prefetcher hitting the venue's approval email would confirm the booking before the venue saw the request. Now GET+token renders a dark-themed confirmation page whose form POSTs to itself; the mutation path unchanged.
  4. **`respond_to_hold_offer` missing pre-book gates** — [services/gig_hold.py:485-542](backend/services/gig_hold.py). Legacy per-gig hold accept ran only time-overlap + freq + Stripe Connect. A banned artist, an artist without a current W-9, or one on a blackout could accept a hold-offer email and land a booking. Now runs the same ban / W-9 / blackout / per-gig-dedupe suite as `book_slot` and `_book_one_series_pick`. Each rejection returns a specific user-facing message.
  5. **Hold-accept atomic dedupe** — [services/gig_hold.py:602-620](backend/services/gig_hold.py). The slot-claim UPDATE gained a `NOT EXISTS` clause so parallel-tab hold accepts by the same artist can't double-slot on the same gig. rowcount=0 now re-queries the slot status to distinguish "someone else took it" vs "you already have another slot here."
  6. **`book_gig` same-day approval slot race** — [routes/gigs.py:2665](backend/routes/gigs.py). The `UPDATE gig_slots SET status='pending_venue_approval'` was the last unguarded slot-promotion path — two racing artists both passed the prior SELECT and both wrote. Added `WHERE status='open'` + rowcount check + 409 SLOT_TAKEN on miss.
  7. **`approve_booking` hardening** — same file. (a) Re-runs `_enforce_no_artist_time_overlap` at approval time because the artist may have booked elsewhere in the interim; (b) clears `last_cancelled_artist_id` on approval commit so the cancelled artist isn't excluded from future blasts; (c) passes `slot_id` to `send_booking_emails` so multi-slot approves don't re-email every previously-booked slot.
  8. **`countersign_contract` multi-slot `gigs.artist_id` leak** — [routes/contracts.py:1763-1783](backend/routes/contracts.py). Multi-slot partial-book path was writing `gigs.artist_id = :aid` even though that column is the single-slot winner field. Now checks `is_multi_slot` and skips the write; `contract_hold_*` cleanup stays.
  9. **`delete_gig_completely` orphan cleanup** — [services/gig_cleanup.py:391-405](backend/services/gig_cleanup.py). Added `gig_cancelled_artists` and `pending_approval_tokens` cleanup so recycled gig IDs can't inherit stale exclusions or replayable approval links.
  10. **`update_recurring_series` shrink guard** — [routes/gigs.py:4098-4115](backend/routes/gigs.py). Added `assert_no_charged_transactions` before `delete_gig_completely` so an unexpected charged/free-trial audit row can't be silently dropped during a series shrink. Matches the guard already in `cancel_gig` / `cancel_slot` / `delete_gig_with_slots`.
  11. **Payout scheduler test-mode multi-slot fix** — [payout_scheduler.py:213-232](backend/payout_scheduler.py). Test transactions flipped only the parent to `transferred` — children stayed at `scheduled` forever (the 2 h auto-settle poll keys on `transferred + test_transfer`, which the un-touched children never hit). Children now sweep alongside the parent. `datetime.utcnow()` stragglers at lines 795 + 814 normalized to `utcnow_naive()`.
  12. **Login lockout DB-backed** — [routes/auth.py:293-408](backend/routes/auth.py), [db.py:942-964](backend/db.py). `_login_attempts` was a module-level dict — under the 2-uvicorn-worker prod topology, worker A didn't see worker B's counter (effective threshold doubled) and every restart wiped state. Now a new `login_attempts(email, ip, attempt_count, locked_until, updated_at)` DB table backs all three helpers (`_check_lockout` / `_record_failed_login` / `_clear_failed_logins`), so counters share across workers and survive restarts. `_clear_failed_logins` also no longer scans the entire in-memory dict on every login — one DELETE with an indexed email lookup.
  13. **`signups_enabled` kill-switch tightening** — [routes/auth.py:429-458](backend/routes/auth.py). The bare `except: pass` swallowed transient DB errors and let signups proceed silently during exactly the outages the kill switch exists for. Now only swallows "table missing" (fresh DB); every other error is re-raised as 503.
  14. **Postgres compat sweep — admin.py** — [routes/admin.py:15-88](backend/routes/admin.py). 19 raw `PRAGMA table_info` / `sqlite_master` calls scattered across admin endpoints would 500 on Postgres. Added three shared helpers (`_list_tables`, `_table_exists`, `_column_info`) that branch on `_IS_POSTGRES` internally, and refactored every call site to use them. The entire admin dashboard, DB browser, and lazy migrations now work on both engines.
  15. **`_sqlite_to_pg` translator gaps** — [db.py:218-247](backend/db.py). Added rewriters for `date/time('now', 'localtime')`, `time(col, '+N unit')`, and `date(col, '+N unit')` — three forms used by the scheduler + open_gig_digest that would have thrown on Postgres.
  16. **`is_admin` literal normalization** — [payout_scheduler.py:1314](backend/payout_scheduler.py) + [stripe_connect.py:2507](backend/routes/stripe_connect.py). Two admin-email-lookup queries used `is_admin = 'true' OR is_admin = 1`. Normalized to `LOWER(COALESCE(CAST(is_admin AS TEXT), 'false')) IN ('true', '1') AND email IS NOT NULL` — matches every legacy form.
  17. **Rate limits on anonymous public endpoints** — added `@limiter.limit("30/minute")` on `POST /api/analytics/track` (write path — anonymous attacker was previously bounded only by the 200/min global default and each call inserted one analytics row); `@limiter.limit("30/minute")` on `GET /api/venues/public`; `@limiter.limit("60/minute")` on `GET /api/venues/{id}/public`, `/api/artists/{id}/gigs/public`, `/api/gigs/{id}/slots/public`. Prevents anonymous bulk-scraping / DB bloat.
  18. **Email footer 19 broken relative links** — [email_templates.py](backend/email_templates.py). Every `href="../"` (which resolves to `mail.google.com/mail/../` inside Gmail) replaced with `href="https://gigsfill.com"`. Verified 50 correct absolute links post-fix, 0 relative.

  **Smoke-tested post-restart**: `/hold/decline/nosuchtoken` GET → 404 (Offer not found), POST → 200 (handled by respond_to_hold_offer error path); `/series-hold/decline/nosuchtoken` GET → 404; `approve-booking` GET+token → confirmation page HTML with `<form>`; `/api/analytics/track` → 200 (rate limiter engaged, not 500); `login_attempts` table created; both services `active` on restart with no errors in logs.

  **Deferred / accepted risk**: `_sqlite_to_pg` full test suite against `DATABASE_URL=postgresql://…` should still be run once before flipping DB. In-code translator now covers every pattern the audits found, but only a live Postgres run gives absolute confidence.

- **2026-06-30 (Y3 — series-hold decline link hardened against link prefetchers + forwards):** `/series-hold/decline/{token}` was a one-click GET with no user dependency. Gmail/Outlook/Slack link prefetchers (which auto-GET URLs in incoming mail to render previews + safety-scan) would silently fire the decline before the artist ever clicked. A forwarded email also let any recipient decline 26 dates on the artist's behalf. Fixed:
  1. **GET path now branches on auth** ([routes/gigs.py:8791](backend/routes/gigs.py)). New `_resolve_series_hold_token` helper looks up `artist_id`, `venue_name`, gig count, and date span via a GROUP-BY on `(offer_token)` — no mutation. If the caller is logged in AND owns the token's artist (`a.user_id == user.id`), execute the decline immediately (status quo for legitimate access). Otherwise return a confirmation page with a POST form — GET no longer mutates state in the un-auth'd case, so link prefetchers can't trigger anything.
  2. **POST `/series-hold/decline/{token}`** (new). Form target for the confirmation page. The actual decline fires here, after a deliberate human click. Same `respond_to_series_hold_offer(action='decline')` body, same response shape.
  3. **Shared HTML helpers** — `_h_esc()` (HTML-escape using stdlib `html.escape`) and `_series_hold_landing_html(title, body, color, status)` (consistent landing-page chrome). Venue/artist names embedded in the confirmation page go through `_h_esc` so a malicious display name can't inject script.

  Verified: unknown-token GET returns 404 ("Offer not found"); unknown-token POST returns 410 (handled by `respond_to_series_hold_offer`'s normal error path). Legacy `/hold/decline/{token}` (per-gig) is intentionally left as-is per the prior audit's "accepted" note — it has a narrower blast radius (one date vs a series) and the same defense pattern can be applied later if needed.

- **2026-06-30 (Punch-list cleanup: scheduler bug + deferred audit YELLOWs + door-deal auto-settle + N+1 cleanup):** Closed every outstanding item in one pass before tomorrow's 7/1 gig.
  1. **Scheduler `ambiguous column name: artist_id`** ([services/gig_hold.py:798](backend/services/gig_hold.py)). Hold-offer advancement was throwing every 10 minutes for ≥6 hours: the expirations query's SELECT had unqualified `offer_token, artist_id, gig_id` while joining `gig_waitlist wl` with `gigs g` — and `gigs.artist_id` (single-slot winner) collides with `gig_waitlist.artist_id`. Table-qualified all three columns (`wl.offer_token`, `wl.artist_id`, `MIN(wl.gig_id)`). Smoke-tested by calling `process_hold_offers(db)` directly — no error. Scheduler restarted, next cycle ran clean.
  2. **Y1 — `update_recurring_series` shrink path** ([routes/gigs.py:4078](backend/routes/gigs.py)). The bespoke 3-statement delete (`flyers` + `gig_slots` + `gigs`) left orphan rows in `gig_contracts`, `notifications`, `gig_email_log`, `public_activity`, `gig_messages`, `waitlist_offered`, etc. Replaced with `delete_gig_completely` (the canonical helper that covers every FK-bearing table). Kept the explicit flyer cleanup (template-aware) before it. Fallback to the legacy 3-statement path if the helper raises, so a single missing-table miss doesn't break the whole shrink.
  3. **Y4 — `hold-mgmt.js` remove-from-waitlist silent failure** ([app/static/js/hold-mgmt.js:471-495](app/static/js/hold-mgmt.js)). `await fetch(...).json()` ignored `res.ok`, so 4xx/5xx responses produced a no-op click. Now checks both the `hold-status` GET and the `hold/reorder` POST, surfaces the FastAPI `{detail}` message via `window.showErrorModal`, and bumped `hold-mgmt.js?v=12` on venue-create-gigs.html.
  4. **Door-deal auto-settle at $0 when venue never files receipts** ([payout_scheduler.py:172](backend/payout_scheduler.py)). Door slots booked at the guarantee carry that amount through to payout via `_create_booking_transaction`'s door anchor (Jun 30 countersign fix). The new behavior closes the audit trail: when the payout scheduler is about to charge for a gig, any door slot with `settled_at IS NULL` is auto-stamped `door_receipts_cents=0, settled_pay_cents=guarantee_cents, settled_at=now()` so the gig_slots row reads "filed at $0 — guarantee only" instead of an indefinite NULL. The transaction amount was already the guarantee so no money math changes — this is the bookkeeping companion. Matches the user's intent: "if user doesn't enter anything after the gig, they get the guarantee, the door split is zero."
  5. **N+1 slot fetches deleted on three public pages** ([static/js/public-gigs.js:531-542 + ~263](app/static/js/public-gigs.js), [app/artist-profile.html:583-591](app/artist-profile.html)). Both pages were fan-out-fetching `/api/gigs/{id}/slots/public` per multi-slot gig at calendar render — wholly redundant since `/api/gigs/public` and `/api/artists/{id}/gigs/public` now embed `slots` directly (today's earlier batched-query change). Replaced the loops with a single in-place sort; cuts cold-load latency on the public discovery pages.
  6. **`/api/artists/{id}/gigs/public` slot-attach** ([routes/gigs.py:1397](backend/routes/gigs.py)). Same batched-SELECT pattern as `/api/gigs/public`. Powers the artist-profile cleanup above + the artist-profile detail modal's `Event:` title row.
  7. **`utcnow_naive` typo** ([routes/gigs.py:5267](backend/routes/gigs.py)). The same-day approval contract auto-execution called `utcnow_naive()` instead of the file's `_utcnow_naive` alias — would NameError if that branch ever fired. Pre-existing latent bug surfaced by the linter; corrected to the prefixed import.

- **2026-06-30 (Artist calendar: "Show All Booked Gigs" modal + CSV/PDF export):** New button to the left of "Export to Calendar (.ics)" on the Artist page calendar toolbar. Opens a wide modal listing every confirmed booking the artist holds, chronologically with closest-to-today first. Columns: Date, Venue (linked to public profile), City/State, Start, End, Pay, Cancel — every header is sortable. Multi-slot gigs expand into one row per booked slot (each slot may carry its own time / pay / deal-summary chip + a small "Slot N" badge). The header includes "📊 Export to Excel (.csv)" and "📄 Export to PDF" buttons:
  1. **`/opt/gigsfill/app/static/js/artist-booked-gigs-modal.js` (new file)**. Reuses the existing `/api/my/gigs?artist_id=X` endpoint (already returns gigs + per-slot data with `pay_summary`) — no new backend route. Expands rows where `gig.status='booked'` (single) OR `slot.artist_id=me AND slot.status='booked'` (multi). Cancel button hits `/api/gigs/{id}/cancel` for single-slot or `/api/gigs/{id}/slots/{sid}/cancel` for multi-slot, threading `cancelled_by='artist'` + the artist's reason through `window.apiDeleteSafe` so FastAPI error messages surface in the styled error modal. Post-cancel: re-fetches modal data and calls `window.refreshArtistGigs()` so the underlying calendar repaints without a page reload.
  2. **CSV export**. Client-side `Blob([...], { type: 'text/csv;charset=utf-8;' })` download (same pattern as `admin-init.js`, `venue-stripe-payment.js`, etc.) — Excel-compatible, no library dependency. Includes Date, Venue, City, State, Start, End, Pay, Title, Slot columns.
  3. **PDF export**. Opens a fresh tab containing the same table styled for `@page { size: landscape; margin: 0.5in; }`, then auto-triggers `window.print()` after a 300 ms render delay. User picks "Save as PDF" from the browser print dialog — works in every browser, no jsPDF dependency for the tabular path. Page header shows generation date + booking count; column widths are tuned so every row fits on one line in landscape.
  4. **Wiring**. [app/artist-book-gigs.html:1565](app/artist-book-gigs.html) — toolbar div changed from `text-align:right` to flex with 8 px gap; new `#artistShowAllBookedBtn` placed to the left of the iCal link, styled identically (same 0.75 rem font, 4 × 10 px padding, dim border that brightens green on hover to match the modal's accent). [app/static/js/artist-book-gigs-init.js:46](app/static/js/artist-book-gigs-init.js) wires the click handler to `window.openArtistBookedGigsModal(artistId)`. `artist-book-gigs-init.js?v=10` bump.

- **2026-06-30 (Public calendar bubbles: per-slot accuracy + gig title surfaced):** User reported the venue Public profile (`/app/venue-profile.html`) showed gig 539 as a single green "Open" bubble even though 2 of 3 slots were booked. Same gig also had a title that never made it into the hover or detail modal. Root cause: `/api/gigs/public` returned `is_multi_slot=1` but no `slots` array, so the venue-profile per-slot expansion path (which keys off `g.slots && g.slots.length > 0`) never fired and fell through to the single-bubble `g.status` branch — and `g.status='open'` is the gig-level status, not the aggregate of all slot statuses. Fixes:
  1. **`/api/gigs/public` now returns `slots` for multi-slot gigs** ([routes/gigs.py:1466](backend/routes/gigs.py)). One batched SELECT (`gig_id IN (...)`) over `gig_slots`, grouped + attached by id. Same redacted shape `/api/gigs/{id}/slots/public` already returns (no `pay`, no `guarantee_cents`, no door receipts). Eliminates the N+1 fan-out public-gigs.js + artist-profile.html were doing client-side for hover hydration.
  2. **Hover card surfaces the venue's gig title** ([static/js/gig-hover-card.js:311 + 367](app/static/js/gig-hover-card.js); [static/css/gig-hover-card.css:36](app/static/css/gig-hover-card.css)). New dimmed italic `.gf-ghc-title` subhead between the status header and date block — only renders if the venue set a title.
  3. **Detail modals show the title as an "Event" row** ([app/venue-profile.html:771](app/venue-profile.html), [app/artist-profile.html:703](app/artist-profile.html), [static/js/public-gigs.js:413](app/static/js/public-gigs.js)). Title-bearing gigs ("Sat Recur Gig", "Acoustic Tuesdays") now read clearly on the public detail modal.
  4. **Cache busters bumped to gig-hover-card.css?v=10 / gig-hover-card.js?v=21** on all 5 pages (artist-book-gigs, artist-profile, public-gigs, venue-create-gigs, venue-profile) so the new title row + per-slot-icon grid render for cached visitors.

- **2026-06-30 (Pre-7/1 audit fixes — frequency-exempt waiver wired end-to-end + series-hold race tuple):** Background audit returned 3 REDs that all centered on `gigs.frequency_exempt`. The auto-waiver `create_hold_waitlist` stamps when a venue intentionally picks an under-freq artist was a no-op end-to-end: every downstream gate ignored the flag and 403'd the artist anyway. Fixes:
  1. **`respond_to_hold_offer` honors `frequency_exempt`** ([services/gig_hold.py:384 + 405-409](backend/services/gig_hold.py)). Loads `COALESCE(frequency_exempt,0)` into `gig_for_checks` and short-circuits the freq gate when truthy. Hold acceptances now respect the venue's explicit waiver — the comment claiming "hold acceptances do NOT waive the policy" was wrong; the waiver originates from the venue's own picker action, not from the artist.
  2. **`_run_prebooking_checks` honors `frequency_exempt`** ([routes/gigs.py:2163](backend/routes/gigs.py)). Was missed when `book_with_contract` was migrated to this helper. Same `_blast_waives = True` short-circuit. Fixes contract-required venues silently rejecting exempt artists.
  3. **`book_slot` honors `frequency_exempt`** ([routes/gigs.py:4813](backend/routes/gigs.py)). Was the original miss — multi-slot booking path was added after `book_gig` and never picked up the exempt branch. Now every booking path (book_gig, book_with_contract → _run_prebooking_checks, book_slot, respond_to_hold_offer) reads the same flag.
  4. **Series-hold race-path return-tuple shape** ([services/gig_hold.py:2253](backend/services/gig_hold.py)). `_book_one_series_pick`'s contract is `(ok, msg, post_action)` but the slot-just-taken branch returned a 2-tuple, causing the caller's unpack to ValueError + rollback with an opaque message instead of "Slot was just taken — please refresh." Padded to 3-tuple.

  **Verified clean** (audit confirmations, no changes needed): door-settle financial pipeline, payout_scheduler defense-in-depth, all `_create_booking_transaction` callers pass `slot_id` for slots (after today's countersign fix), `cleanup_hold_records` wired into both `cancel_gig` + `delete_gig_with_slots` keep_open paths, series-extend hold propagation via `create_hold_waitlist(defer_kickoff=True)`, frequency operator `<=` consistent across all 6 callsites, calendar `.day.other-month` separation, atomic series-bulk-book rollback semantics. Two YELLOWs deferred to next pass: `update_recurring_series` shrink-path orphan rows (low risk — parent gig must be open/cancelled), `hold-mgmt.js:471` JSON parse without ok check (silent click-no-op already wrapped in try/catch).

- **2026-06-30 (Countersign door-deal override bypass — real-money fix):** While verifying a real booked gig (id=539, 7/1) the user's payment math was inconsistent: Fifty Proof's door slot showed `guarantee_cents=1000` ($10), `apply_override=0`, slot.pay=$10 — yet her `artist_payout` transaction `amount_cents=1500` ($15). Root cause: `routes/contracts.py:countersign_contract` called `_create_booking_transaction(...)` WITHOUT `slot_id`. With no slot_id, the door-deal anchor branch ([gigs.py:482](backend/routes/gigs.py#L482)) was skipped and `_get_effective_pay()` ran, which applied the preferred-artist `pay_dollars_override=$15` to a door deal — exactly what `apply_override=0` is supposed to forbid. Net effect: venue would have been over-charged $5 and Fifty Proof over-paid $5 before /settle could correct it. Fixes:
  1. **Pass slot_id at countersign** ([routes/contracts.py:1812](backend/routes/contracts.py)): countersign now looks up every booked slot for `(gig_id, artist_id)` and calls `_create_booking_transaction` once per slot, threading each slot's id through. Multi-slot artists on the same gig now get one txn per slot (each can have its own deal_type/guarantee). Fallback to the old no-slot call only if the lookup returns zero rows.
  2. **Production data repair**: TXN 341 (Fifty Proof, gig 539) corrected to `amount_cents=1000`; `_recompute_gig_fees(539)` re-normalized the parent + sibling so the final scheduled state is `$20 base → $25 venue charge → $7.50/$7.50 payouts → $10 commission split 50/50`. Door % share will layer in at /settle as designed.

- **2026-07-03 (Blast emails: artist_type filter on every path):** User reported a Live Band artist getting a 36h blast for a Comedian gig. Root cause: the preferred-artists branch of the scheduler had NO artist_type filter — every preferred artist at the venue got blasted regardless of whether their type matched the gig. Same gap (for multi-slot mixed-type gigs) existed in 3 other blast paths. Fixed everywhere.
