  1. **`scheduler.process_open_gig_notifications` preferred-artists** ([scheduler.py:682](backend/scheduler.py)): added SQL filter — artist's `artist_type` must match either gig-level `artist_type` OR any OPEN slot's `artist_type` on multi-slot gigs.
  2. **`scheduler.process_open_gig_notifications` blast_all** ([scheduler.py:796 area](backend/scheduler.py)): computes accepted-types set (gig-level + open slot types) once per gig; Python-side filter skips artists whose type isn't in the set. Replaces the previous gig-level-only check which under-blasted multi-slot mixed gigs.
  3. **`scheduler.process_radius_blast`** ([scheduler.py:977 area](backend/scheduler.py)): same accepted-types set pattern. Previously only checked gig-level type.
  4. **`fire_cancelled_gig_blast`** ([routes/gigs.py:6432 + 6620](backend/routes/gigs.py)): both the preferred-artists branch and the radius-fallback branch now use the same accepted-types set. Multi-slot cancellations no longer skip preferred artists matching the OTHER slot type.

  **Verified clean (no defect):**
  - **Hold-offer email send** (`_send_hold_offer_email`): goes only to the artist whose row matched the hold rotation. Type already enforced at queue-build time + via `_list_matching_open_slots`.
  - **Series-hold email** (`_send_series_hold_offer_email`): goes only to artists in the hold queue; their eligible list is filtered by `_artist_matches_slot`.
  - **Daily digest** (`open_gig_digest.py:_fetch_artist_open_gigs_live`): explicit type filter at line 484-489.
  - **Waitlist offer** (`notify_waitlist`): waitlist join already enforces type match at the join endpoint.

- **2026-07-03 (Phase 5 final audit fix bundle):** Background audit agent ran a deep dive after the user reported real-world bugs. Returned 6 RED + 9 YELLOW findings. Plus one I caught independently (modal-vs-backend `< freq_days` vs `<= freq_days` inconsistency). Closed everything out.
  1. **`generate_auto_contract` signature** (RED #1). Series helper was calling it with `(db, gig_id, artist_id)` — actual signature is `(db, gig_id, venue_id, artist_id, slot_id=None)`. The `try/except` swallowed the TypeError and stored `rendered_body=""` on every series contract. Fixed: pass `venue_id` + `slot_id` correctly.
  2. **`hold_expires_at` on gig_contracts** (RED #2). Series contracts were inserted with `hold_expires_at=NULL`, so the expired-holds sweep `_cleanup_expired_holds_impl` (which only reaps rows where `hold_expires_at < now`) skipped them forever. Slots stuck in `awaiting_venue_contract` permanently. Fixed: stamp 48h countersign deadline + set `gigs.contract_hold_artist_id` and `gigs.contract_hold_expires_at` so the canonical sweep can wind down stalled series contracts.
  3. **Venue notifications + emails after artist signs series contract** (RED #3). The series sign path skipped `_create_booking_notifications` and `send_contract_sign_email` — venue had no idea contracts were awaiting countersign. Fixed: post-commit replay fires both per pick.
  4. **GET `/series-hold/decline/{token}` landing route** (RED #4). Email's "Decline All" button linked to a non-existent endpoint, returning 404. Added the GET handler that calls `respond_to_series_hold_offer(action='decline')` and returns a friendly confirmation page.
  5. **Atomic bulk-book loop** (RED #5 + #6). `_create_booking_transaction` commits internally, so a failed pick #2 couldn't roll back pick #1's already-committed transactions, booking emails, and activity notifications. Refactored `_book_one_series_pick` to do ONLY in-transaction state changes (slot UPDATE, contract INSERT, gig status promotion) and return a `post_action` descriptor. `respond_to_series_hold_offer` collects descriptors, COMMITS once at the end, then replays the post-commit actions (transactions, emails, notifications). Any mid-loop failure now rolls back the whole batch cleanly.
  6. **Blast token + last_cancelled_artist_id cleanup** (YELLOW #1). Series accept now clears these on the parent gig, matching the per-gig hold path.
  7. **Series-aware decline notification** (YELLOW #2). New `_notify_venue_decline_series` helper sends one email saying *"declined the series of N dates (first–last)"* instead of misleadingly naming a single date. Wired into the decline action, the artist-late-click expiry path, and the scheduler expiry sweep.
  8. **Email respects venue's `hold_offer_window_hours`** (YELLOW #3). Bundled email previously hardcoded "24 hours". Now interpolates `{{window_label}}` (e.g. "48 hours" or "2 days") from the venue's actual setting.
  9. **Series-bucket exhaustion notification** (YELLOW #4). When `_send_next_series_offer_for_type` runs out of candidates AND no other bucket has an in-flight or queued offer, flag every series gig with `hold_status='exhausted'` and fire a single venue activity-center notification: *"Series hold exhausted — N dates need action."*
  10. **Stripe Connect hard-block** (YELLOW #5). Series helper was logging a warning when the artist had no Stripe Connect onboarding; now hard-blocks in live-payments mode (matches `/hold/accept` behavior). Test-mode venues are unaffected.
  11. **Skip backend frequency check in series helper** (YELLOW #6). `_run_prebooking_checks` runs `<= freq_days` against the artist's existing bookings — but inside a multi-pick loop, pick #1's just-inserted slot would block pick #2 against itself. The modal's frequency-greying is authoritative; backend now only runs the gates that don't depend on within-batch state (ban / W-9 / blackout / cross-venue time-overlap). The other gates' guarantees are preserved through the modal's pre-emptive greying + the per-gig dedupe rule.
  12. **Banner consistency** (YELLOW #7). `/api/me/hold-offers` now excludes ALL gigs with `recurring_group_id IS NOT NULL` (cleaner than the count-based heuristic). `/api/me/series-hold-offers` collapses on `(offer_token, artist_id)` regardless of date count — single-date series shows up as a series card, not double-rendered as both per-gig and series.
  13. **Modal vs backend frequency rule consistency** (my own catch). Frontend used `gap < freq_days` (allow at equal), backend uses `gap <= freq_days` (block at equal). User could pick a date the backend then rejected. Modal now matches backend: `gap <= freq_days` greys out, with tooltip *"venue needs more than N between bookings"*.
  14. **Audit-verified clean** (no changes needed): kickoff URL fix, PDF + Hold + Recurring rejection at create-time, series-extend hold propagation, atomic slot-claim race (`WHERE status='open'` rowcount), Postgres-safe timestamps, JS composite-key dedupe, hash deep-link auto-open, `window.openSeriesHoldModal` exposed, modal redirect intercept in `openGigModal`, all six JS cache versions bumped.

- **2026-07-02 (Phase 5 audit follow-up — all remaining deferreds + edge cases):** Closed out the remaining YELLOWs from the morning audit + walked the flow end-to-end to surface every state-consistency edge case.
  1. **Out-of-series frequency greying** (audit #11). New helper `_list_artist_other_bookings_at_venue` returns this artist's existing bookings at this venue OUTSIDE the series. The offer endpoint now passes them to the modal as `existing_bookings`. `_computeGreyed` greys series dates within `freq_days` of any pre-existing one-off with a clear tooltip: *"You already have a gig at this venue on Aug 5 — only 7 days apart (venue needs ≥28)."* Three-rule cascade: same-gig dedupe first, then existing-booking conflicts, then within-modal pick conflicts.
  2. **Post-create series-extend hold propagation** (audit #12). [routes/gigs.py:update_recurring_series](backend/routes/gigs.py) now detects whether the series has a live hold (any instance with `hold_status='active'` + hold-source waitlist rows). If yes, every new gig inserted by the extend operation gets the SAME ordered artist list staged via `create_hold_waitlist` with `defer_kickoff=True`. New dates join the hold; the next-in-line artist's bundled email naturally picks them up via `_list_series_eligible_gigs` on the next bucket advance.
  3. **Late contract toggle re-prompt** (audit #13). If the venue activates a contract template after the artist's modal already loaded, the bulk-book POST returns `needs_signature: true`. The modal now catches that response, re-fetches the offer (to pick up the new `requires_contract: true` + venue_name), and opens the contract sub-modal to collect the signature instead of showing a generic "Booking failed."
  4. **Venue notifications on decline + late-click expiry** (audit #14, #15). `respond_to_series_hold_offer` now calls `_notify_venue_decline(reason='declined')` on Decline All and `_notify_venue_decline(reason='expired')` on the late-click expiry branch. The scheduler sweep at `process_hold_offers` was already grouping by token from the morning pass; the artist-initiated late-click path is the second backstop.
  5. **Freq-exempt model clarified** (audit #16). Series mode intentionally does NOT auto-set `frequency_exempt=1` even when the venue picked under-freq artists. The chain of `#11 greying + sequential booking + canonical pre-book gates` enforces frequency correctly: existing bookings grey out pre-emptively; each accepted pick becomes the next pick's "existing booking" so within-series freq conflicts are caught server-side too. Nothing to override.
  6. **Auth-protected offer detail endpoint** (audit #19). `GET /api/series-hold/offer/{token}` now requires the caller to own the artist the token resolves to (matches the POST endpoint's auth shape). Token-as-auth pattern dropped — an email-forward leak no longer exposes 26 dates of venue/artist/pay info.
  7. **Activity Center fan-out on bulk-book**. `_book_one_series_pick` no-contract path now calls `notify_gig_booked` so both venue and artist entity_users see each new booking in their notification dropdown — same pattern as the canonical book_slot path.
  8. **Hold pill + series hint visibility synced on both toggles**. Toggling Recurring updates the in-Hold-panel series hint; toggling Hold on while Recurring is already on shows the hint immediately. No state-getting-stuck-stale on either order.
  9. **Per-date cancel from series-booked** verified to work via the existing `cancel_slot` endpoint with no changes — the slot just reopens, other series dates untouched, transaction cleanup runs through the canonical helpers.

  **What's verified clean:** RED #1 (transaction created), #2 (one expiry email), #3 (one reminder), #4 (all pre-book gates run), #6 (PDF contracts refused early), #7 (composite key dedupe both frontend + backend), #8 (banner shows series collapsed); YELLOW #9 (hold_status clears), #20 (Postgres-safe timestamps), #22 (door-deal override writes both fields), and the cross-venue overlap check via `_enforce_no_artist_time_overlap`.

- **2026-07-02 (Phase 5 audit fix bundle):** A deep-dive audit found 8 RED + 14 YELLOW issues in the Phase 5 ship from yesterday. Fixed every RED + the show-stopper YELLOWs in one pass.
  1. **`_book_one_series_pick` rewritten** ([services/gig_hold.py:1770](backend/services/gig_hold.py)) to delegate to canonical helpers instead of re-implementing booking. Now runs the same pre-book gates `book_slot` runs (ban / preferred / W-9 / frequency / blackout via `_run_prebooking_checks`; `_enforce_no_artist_time_overlap` for cross-venue conflicts; per-gig dedupe enforcing one-slot-per-artist-per-gig). Calls `_create_booking_transaction` for the no-contract path (fixes the SILENT money bug: artists were getting booked without any transaction row → venue never charged, artist never paid). Fires `send_booking_emails` on success so artist + venue get confirmation. Refuses `pdf_upload` / `per_gig_pdf` contract venues with a clear message (bundled bulk signing doesn't fit the download-and-upload PDF lifecycle). The slot UPDATE now includes `WHERE status = 'open'` + rowcount check to win the race against concurrent bookings. For door-deal slots with `apply_override=1`, both `pay` AND `guarantee_cents` get written so `_create_booking_transaction`'s door-deal branch reads the override (was silently dropping the override before). Clears `gigs.hold_status` when the gig fully books so the scheduler stops scanning it. Single change kills RED #1, #4, #6, #7, #22 and YELLOW #9.
  2. **Series-aware expiry sweep + reminder sweep** ([services/gig_hold.py:process_hold_offers](backend/services/gig_hold.py)). Previously iterated per-row — a 26-week series with one un-responding artist would fire 26 venue decline emails, 26 next-artist offer emails, and 26 reminder emails. Now `GROUP BY (offer_token, artist_id)` so each bundled offer fires ONE reminder, ONE decline, ONE bucket advance. Series advancement routes through `_advance_series_bucket_for_artist`; per-gig advancement still routes through `send_next_hold_offer`. Kills RED #2, #3.
  3. **Legacy banner filters out series rows** ([routes/gigs.py:/api/me/hold-offers](backend/routes/gigs.py)). The per-gig Pending Offers banner used to render 26 cards for a 26-week series; clicking any card fired the legacy single-gig accept which only booked one slot and orphaned the other 25 rows. Now excludes rows whose `offer_token` is shared across multiple gigs. New sibling endpoint `/api/me/series-hold-offers` returns one collapsed entry per shared-token group; banner JS renders a single purple "Series offer · N dates available · [Pick Your Dates →]" card per series that opens the bundled modal. Kills RED #8.
  4. **Postgres compat** — replaced SQLite-only `datetime('now', '+/- X hours')` in `_send_next_series_offer_for_type`, `send_next_hold_offer`, and `process_hold_offers` reminder query with Python-computed `_dt.utcnow() + _td(hours=...)` strings. The `_PgCompatConn` shim only rewrites `?`→`%s`, not SQL functions, so the legacy code would have thrown on Postgres prod. Kills YELLOW #20.
  5. **Modal dedupe by composite key** ([app/static/js/series-hold-modal.js](app/static/js/series-hold-modal.js)). Selection set previously keyed by `gig_id` only — a multi-type gig (LB + DJ slots) would check BOTH slot rows when the user ticked one, send both `{gig_id, slot_id}` picks, and book the artist into both slots. Now keyed by `${gig_id}_${slot_id}` and the `_computeGreyed` helper applies a SAME-GIG rule (picking any slot on a gig greys out other slots on that gig with explicit *"only one slot per gig"* tooltip) before the FREQUENCY rule. Kills RED #7 on the frontend.
  6. **`frequency_exempt=1` already skipped in series mode** (from previous turn) plus venue picker copy reads correctly *"…their bundled-offer modal will grey out dates that conflict"* instead of the single-gig waiver language.

  **Deferred to a follow-up pass** (recoverable, not test-blocking): YELLOW #11 (modal doesn't grey dates that conflict with the artist's existing OUT-OF-series bookings at the venue), #12 (post-create series edits desync hold), #13 (late contract toggle), #14/#15 (silent decline/expiry — venue gets no notification), #16 (freq-exempt opt-in is half-built — series flow refuses under-freq picks via the new backend gate, so venue can't "force" anyway), #19 (unauth offer detail endpoint — same security shape as legacy hold landing, token-as-auth pattern).

- **2026-07-01 (Phase 5 — Series bulk-apply hold):** Venue can now apply a single hold list across every gig in a recurring series. Each artist gets ONE bundled email listing every date; they tick the dates they can play (frequency-conflicting dates auto-grey with explanation) and sign once. Single signature covers every selected date.
  1. **Frontend force-disable removed** ([app/static/js/venue.create-gigs.js](app/static/js/venue.create-gigs.js)): the Hold pill no longer toggles off when Recurring is checked. New yellow hint inside the Hold panel explains series mode when both are on. The recurring-loop POST now sends `hold_artist_ids` / `hold_email_artists` / `hold_offer_window_hours` per instance, then fires a single `POST /api/series/{rgid}/hold/start` after the loop to launch the bundled offers.
  2. **Backend create path** ([routes/gigs.py:1163 area](backend/routes/gigs.py)): `create_hold_waitlist` gained `defer_kickoff: bool = False`. When `is_recurring=1`, every instance stages the same waitlist (and sets `hold_status='active'` for hidden-from-public-search behavior) but DOES NOT auto-fire its per-gig rotation. The series-start endpoint drives the single bundled fan-out.
  3. **Series rotation service** ([services/gig_hold.py — appended](backend/services/gig_hold.py)). New `start_series_hold`, `_send_next_series_offer_for_type`, `_list_series_eligible_gigs`, `respond_to_series_hold_offer`, `_book_one_series_pick`, `_advance_series_bucket_for_artist`, `_send_series_hold_offer_email`. The shared `offer_token` value is stamped on EVERY gig_waitlist row an artist owns in the series — single token resolves to all rows. Parallel-per-artist-type rotation is preserved within the series, mirroring the single-gig design (Live Band bucket and DJ bucket fire in parallel; each bucket finds its lowest-position un-offered artist by `MIN(position)` across the series's rows).
  4. **Endpoints added** ([routes/gigs.py](backend/routes/gigs.py)):
     - `POST /api/series/{recurring_group_id}/hold/start` — venue-only kickoff. Idempotent (in-flight check skips re-firing).
     - `GET /series-hold/respond/{token}` — HTML landing for the email link, redirects to `/app/artist-book-gigs.html#series-hold=TOKEN`.
     - `GET /api/series-hold/offer/{token}` — modal data: artist + venue + frequency_days + requires_contract + the artist's eligible (open, type-matching) gig list with effective per-slot pay.
     - `POST /api/series-hold/respond/{token}` — body `{action: 'accept'|'decline', slot_picks?: [{gig_id, slot_id}], signature_name?}`. Bulk-book is atomic — any single conflict rolls the lot back. Unselected eligible dates get marked declined on this artist's behalf. Bucket advances after every response (accept-partial, decline-all, or expiry).
  5. **Bundled email template** ([email_templates.py](backend/email_templates.py)): new `hold_series_offer_artist` — single email with table of every eligible date for the artist, Pick / Decline-All CTAs, frequency-rule note. Fans out to artist entity_users.
  6. **Artist-side modal** ([app/static/js/series-hold-modal.js — new file](app/static/js/series-hold-modal.js); included on artist-book-gigs.html via `defer`). Hash-deep-link `#series-hold=TOKEN` auto-opens the modal on page load. Each row is a checkbox: date · time · pay. Picking a date greys out OTHER dates within the venue's frequency window with explicit per-row tooltip *"Frequency conflict — only N days from your selected `<date>` pick (venue needs ≥X)."* Live footer shows count + total pay + how many got greyed. Contract sub-modal opens if the venue requires one — single signature input covers every selected date in one POST; backend creates N `gig_contracts` rows each pre-stamped with the same signature.
  7. **No DB schema changes.** Shared series token re-uses the existing `gig_waitlist.offer_token` column (no UNIQUE constraint on it for that table).

- **2026-06-29 (Hold rotation — parallel per artist-type, Option A + per-bucket UX):** The hold rotation was strictly sequential — one offer in flight at a time across the whole gig. For a multi-type gig (Live Band + DJ slots) with the queue ordered as [LB1, LB2, LB3, DJ1], the DJ slot sat dormant until every Live Band had cycled through. Replaced with one rotation per artist_type bucket so independent slot types fire in parallel, and added per-bucket UX in the venue's hold-management panel.
  1. **`send_next_hold_offer` rewritten** ([services/gig_hold.py:149](backend/services/gig_hold.py)). Now returns `list[int]` (previously `Optional[int]`). For each DISTINCT open-slot artist_type, checks (a) is there an in-flight offer to that type → skip; otherwise (b) find the lowest-position waitlist artist matching that type → offer. Up to 4 parallel offers in practice (Live Band, DJ, Comedian, Trivia Host). Position order is preserved WITHIN each bucket. `_mark_exhausted_and_notify` only fires when EVERY bucket has no in-flight AND no candidates. Existing auto-decline path on band_formats/styles mismatch within the same artist_type still works — its recursion now advances only that bucket.
  2. **`/api/gigs/{gid}/hold-status` returns per-bucket state** ([routes/gigs.py:7964 area](backend/routes/gigs.py)). New `buckets` array, one entry per distinct open-slot artist_type: `{artist_type, slot_count, slot_ids, in_flight: {...} | null, remaining_candidates, state}` where `state` is `'active'` (in-flight or candidates remain), `'exhausted'` (artists of this type were queued but all declined/expired), or `'no_candidates'` (no artist of this type was ever queued — venue forgot).
  3. **New endpoint `POST /api/gigs/{gid}/hold/cancel-slots-by-type`** ([routes/gigs.py — above resolve-exhausted](backend/routes/gigs.py)). Body `{artist_type: 'Live Band'}`. Cancels every open slot of the given type on a held gig; booked slots untouched; other-type rotations keep running. If cancelling drains all open slots gig-wide, flips `gig.status` to `'booked'`/`'cancelled'` and clears `hold_status`. Idempotent.
  4. **`skip-current` caller updated** ([routes/gigs.py:8123](backend/routes/gigs.py)) to handle the new list return from `send_next_hold_offer`. Returns both `next_artist_id` (legacy single id — first in list) and `next_artist_ids` (full list).
  5. **Frontend hold-management panel — per-bucket cards** ([app/static/js/hold-mgmt.js](app/static/js/hold-mgmt.js)). Replaced the single "Currently offered to" line with one card per bucket. Active buckets show the in-flight artist + countdown. `no_candidates` buckets render a red-tinted notice: *"No `<type>` artists on your hold list — this slot won't fill through the hold rotation."* with [+ Add `<type>` artist] + [Cancel `<type>` slot(s)] buttons. `exhausted` buckets similar copy: *"Every `<type>` artist on your list declined or didn't respond in time."* The "+ Add" button opens the existing add-artist picker (which already filters by slot type). Cancel button hits the new bucket-cancel endpoint with a confirmation modal. When ALL buckets are dead, `gigs.hold_status` flips to `'exhausted'` and the existing combined "Open empty slots to all" / "Cancel empty slots" buttons render at the gig level — one click handles everything left.
  6. **Picker hint copy updated** ([app/venue-create-gigs.html:3362](app/venue-create-gigs.html)) to *"Only artists matching at least one slot artist type above are listed. Artists are invited one by one, in the order on the left."* — reflects the new parallel-per-type semantics ("in the order on the left" is per-bucket within the queue order).

- **2026-06-26 (Frequency-waiver transparency on both sides):** Two-part UX feature so the frequency rule's behavior is visible to both artist and venue.
  1. **Artist gig modal — soft yellow "Frequency Rule Lifted" banner.** Surfaces when the artist WOULD be frequency-blocked at this venue but the blast window / `radius_blast_token` / `gigs.frequency_exempt` is waiving the rule. Tells them their last gig here was N days ago vs the venue's required X, names the reason ('window' = open_gig_36h/open_gig_1w fired; 'blast' = radius blast in progress; 'exempt' = venue marked the specific gig exempt). Backend extension in [routes/gig_modal.py](backend/routes/gig_modal.py) computes a new `freq_waiver` object next to the existing `freq_check`; if a closest-booking diff would normally block but the waiver path is taken, populate it instead. Frontend banner in [gig-modal.js](app/static/js/gig-modal.js).
  2. **Venue Hold-Gig artist picker — per-artist freq chip + auto-waiver.** [routes/venues.py](backend/routes/venues.py) `/api/venues/{venue_id}/preferred-artists` accepts an optional `for_gig_date=YYYY-MM-DD` query param. When provided, each artist row carries a `freq_status` object (`applies`, `freq_days`, `under_limit`, `last_gig_date`, `days_between`, `abs_days_between`). Both pickers — [hold-mgmt.js](app/static/js/hold-mgmt.js) (post-creation Add Artists) and [venue.create-gigs.js](app/static/js/venue.create-gigs.js) (create-time Hold panel) — render a yellow chip `freq Nd` next to each under-freq artist plus a one-line yellow summary at top: *"X artists are under this venue's frequency policy. Adding them will waive the rule for this gig only."* The create-time picker also re-fetches when the venue changes the gig date so chips stay accurate. **Auto-waiver wiring**: [services/gig_hold.py:create_hold_waitlist](backend/services/gig_hold.py) checks every picked artist against the venue's frequency policy at the gig date and, if any are under-limit, sets `gigs.frequency_exempt = 1` so the accept-side gate doesn't reject them when their offer fires. Both call sites (create-time and `/hold/start`) benefit — the helper is the single source of truth. Idempotent. `/api/gigs/{id}/hold-status` now also returns `date` so the post-creation picker can request enriched data.

- **2026-06-26 (Overnight payment + payout pipeline audit fixes):** Eight findings from a fresh `stripe_connect` + `payout_scheduler` audit — three RED (money-moving), five YELLOW (state/compliance). All verified against the source before fixing.
  1. **`reinstate_gig_payment` Transfer.create was missing idempotency + source_transaction.** [routes/stripe_connect.py:1624](backend/routes/stripe_connect.py). A network retry could pay the artist twice; absent `source_transaction`, the artist's funds became payable to their bank before the reinstate charge had settled, exposing the platform to chargeback. Fix mirrors the canonical scheduler pattern at `payout_scheduler.py:946-952`: retrieve the PaymentIntent's `latest_charge` and pass `idempotency_key=f"reinstate_{txn_id}_transfer"` + `source_transaction=charge_id` on the `Transfer.create`.
  2. **`reinstate_gig_payment` flipped status to `'paid'` immediately AND lost affiliate commission.** Same function. The canonical state machine reserves `'paid'` for "money landed in artist bank" — the scheduler's transfer-poll loop is what flips `'transferred'` → `'paid'`. The legacy flip overstated the artist's "Total Earned" KPI and (because no affiliate accrual fired) permanently dropped the inviting affiliate's commission on every reinstated charge. Test mode was even worse: it wrote `stripe_transfer_id='test_reinstated'`, which `_settle_test_transactions` (keys on `'test_transfer'`) never matched, so test rows stayed `'paid'` forever. Fix: status → `'transferred'` (both live and test paths), `stripe_transfer_id` → `'test_transfer'` in test mode, and `accrue_affiliate_earnings(db, txn_id)` fires on successful transfer.
  3. **`cancel_gig_payment` left artist_payout child rows in `'scheduled'`.** [routes/stripe_connect.py:858](backend/routes/stripe_connect.py). Defense-in-depth parent-status guard in `payout_scheduler.py` blocked transfer, but the children showed up in the artist's "upcoming payouts" forever. Added the sibling-cancellation UPDATE that `cancel_slot_payment` already does at line 1213, scoped to `gig_id` + `transaction_type='artist_payout'` + still in `'scheduled'/'test'`. State-cleanup only — no money risk, the guard already prevented transfer.
  4. **Test transactions accrued real affiliate earnings.** [payout_scheduler.py:201](backend/payout_scheduler.py). The is_test branch called `accrue_affiliate_earnings` even though no platform commission had actually been earned (no real money moved). If `payments_enabled` ever flipped off→on, accrued test cents would land in the next quarterly affiliate payout as real dollars. Fix: drop the accrual from the test branch — real-money accrual still fires in the LIVE branch at line 700.
  5. **`_create_booking_transaction` ghost child on post-charge parent.** [routes/gigs.py:654](backend/routes/gigs.py). A blast-email booking arriving after the parent venue_charge had already been charged would: (a) find the existing parent via `status NOT IN ('payment_cancelled')`, (b) insert a `'scheduled'` child for the new artist, (c) `_recompute_gig_fees` would no-op (correctly — parent already charged). Result: parent total short by the new artist's pay AND the child stranded outside both scheduler queries. If the stalled-transfer loop ever picked it up it would transfer money the parent never raised, eating the platform's Connect balance. Fix: re-SELECT the parent status before insert; raise HTTP 409 with a clear support-route message if it's past `'scheduled'`/`'test'`.
  6. **Affiliate 1099 threshold check was dead on Postgres.** [routes/affiliate.py:1238](backend/routes/affiliate.py). `strftime('%Y', ap.paid_at)` is SQLite-only; on production Postgres it threw, and the bare `except: pass` silently swallowed it. Compliance risk: admin never warned when an affiliate crossed the IRS-reportable threshold. Fix: explicit calendar-year range (`paid_at >= '{year}-01-01' AND paid_at < '{year+1}-01-01'`) — works on both engines; the bare except now logs the error so future failures surface.
  7. **`reinstate_slot_payment` LIKE-fallback could mis-match.** [routes/stripe_connect.py:1410](backend/routes/stripe_connect.py). The `OR notes NOT LIKE 'Slot %'` branch was meant to catch legacy single-row transactions but on a multi-slot gig with multiple legacy cancelled rows for the same artist (both `slot_id=NULL`, neither note mentioning a slot), `ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1` silently reinstated whichever was most recent — not the one the venue clicked. Fix: drop that branch. Modern rows are still found via `slot_id` FK; legacy rows that explicitly name the slot in notes are still found via the existing LIKE patterns; legacy rows with no disambiguator are now refused so admin can handle manually.
  8. **Verified clean:** defense-in-depth parent-status guard in `payout_scheduler.py:350-375`, initial child status `'scheduled'` (not `'pending_transfer'`) at booking, deterministic venue-charge idempotency keys, atomic CLAIM via `UPDATE WHERE status=expected`, `_apply_slot_pay_override` door-deal logic, `_recompute_gig_fees` safety gate, webhook signature verification + event dedup, `charge.refunded` cancels still-scheduled children + affiliate clawback, admin refund flow, `cancel_slot_payment` parent-cleanup-when-empty, `accrue_affiliate_earnings` uses `commission_cents` not `amount_cents`. No bugs found in any of those paths.

- **2026-06-26 (Overnight cancellation + notification audit fixes):** Pre-test pass before the user's morning full-flow run on the July 19 3-slot gig.
  1. **Hold cleanup on cancel paths.** New `cleanup_hold_records(db, gig_id, *, delete_rows=False)` in [services/gig_cleanup.py](backend/services/gig_cleanup.py). Wired into the keep_open branches of `cancel_gig` ([routes/gigs.py:2906](backend/routes/gigs.py)) and `delete_gig_with_slots` ([routes/gigs.py:5773](backend/routes/gigs.py)) — the venue-UI Cancel Gig button uses the latter. Without this, cancelling a held gig left `hold_status='active'/'exhausted'` so the gig stayed hidden from public search forever, AND a stale email-offer link could still try to book a slot that no longer existed. Helper: (a) marks every in-flight `gig_waitlist` row with `source='hold'`, `offer_sent=1`, `offer_declined=0` as declined + expired; (b) sets `gigs.hold_status = NULL`; (c) if `delete_rows=True` (full delete paths only — those already go through `delete_gig_completely` which inlines the same DELETE), drops the hold-source `gig_waitlist` rows + `waitlist_offered` tokens. Idempotent. cancel_slot left alone — it's per-slot and shouldn't kill a hold cycle still active on sibling slots.
  2. **pending_approval_tokens cleanup on keep_open reopen.** Mirror of the existing full-delete cleanup. `cancel_gig` keep_open branch now drops the cancelled artist's tokens (only — sibling artists' approvals stay live); `delete_gig_with_slots` keep_open drops gig-wide tokens (every booked artist on the gig was just cancelled). Without this, an artist whose slot was reopened could still click their stale email approval link and trigger a "no pending approval" error.
  3. **Hold-offer email now fans out to artist entity_users.** [services/gig_hold.py:_send_hold_offer_email](backend/services/gig_hold.py) was emailing only the artist row's primary user; secondary band members who handle the inbox missed the entire offer cycle. Now uses `get_all_entity_users(db, "artist", artist_id)` and emails every distinct address; falls back to the primary email if entity-user lookup returns nothing.
  4. **Waitlist-offer email same fix.** [routes/waitlist.py:916](backend/routes/waitlist.py) was emailing `entry["email"]` only; now fans to every artist entity user with the same fallback pattern.
  5. **Audit false-alarm confirmed and recorded.** The "venue countersign skips artist confirmation email" finding was a misread of [routes/contracts.py:1823](backend/routes/contracts.py) — `send_booking_emails` IS called inside the countersign success path, and that helper does fan out to both venue and artist entity_users via `get_all_entity_users`. No code change needed.

- **2026-06-24 (Hold accept ↔ contract signing integration + audit-driven fixes):** Overnight pass — implemented the deferred contract integration end-to-end and shipped seven fixes from three parallel audit sweeps.
  1. **Hold-accept routes through contract signing.** Backend: extended [POST /api/gigs/{gig_id}/book-with-contract](backend/routes/contracts.py) to accept an optional `hold_token` body/query param. New helper `_hold_cleanup_after_contract_book` (in contracts.py) is called before each of the three book-with-contract return points (digital `'booked'`, per-gig PDF `'awaiting_venue_contract'`, template PDF `'pending_contract'`); it locates the artist's hold-waitlist row (by token or by source='hold' + offer_sent=1 + offer_declined=0), marks it consumed, and either calls `send_next_hold_offer` (slots remaining) or clears `hold_status` (gig fully booked). Frontend: [hold-offers-banner.js](app/static/js/hold-offers-banner.js) `_accept` is now `_acceptWithContractCheck` — it fetches `/api/venues/{vid}/contracts/active`; if `has_contract && require_for_booking`, it stashes `window._pendingSlotBooking = { gigId, slotId, artistId, holdToken }` and opens the appropriate signing modal (`showContractSigningModal` / `showPdfContractModal` / `showPerGigPdfModal`) — all three are now exposed globally from artist.book-gigs.js. The signing modals were extended to thread `hold_token` into the `/book-with-contract` body. No-contract path stays on the existing `/hold/accept` fast-path. Same contract data attrs (`data-gig`, `data-vid`, `data-aid`) added to the gig-modal's Pending Offer Book button so the in-modal Book click flows through the same logic.
  2. **`/hold/accept/{token}` now content-negotiates** ([gigs.py:7522](backend/routes/gigs.py)). Returns JSON `{ok, message, slot_id}` when `Accept: application/json` (the banner uses this), or HTML landing page otherwise (the email link's multi-slot picker form). Non-ok JSON returns HTTP 400 so the banner's `res.ok` flips false in addition to the explicit `{ok: false}` body check. Banner `_accept` now parses the body when content-type is JSON and surfaces `payload.message` (e.g. "Your payout account isn't set up yet — connect Stripe…") in the error modal instead of the generic "Something went wrong — please refresh".
  3. **Frequency-conflict error message** in `respond_to_hold_offer` now matches `book_slot` — includes direction ("You have a gig before/after this one on …") AND the days-needed gap ("Gigs must be at least N more day(s) apart"). Previously just said "You're already booked on DATE" with no remediation hint.
  4. **`book_slot` now refuses to book a held gig** ([routes/gigs.py:4447 area](backend/routes/gigs.py)). Reads `gigs.hold_status` and raises 403 with a clear message ("This gig is currently held — bookings must come through your Pending Offers banner…") if active or exhausted. Defense-in-depth — held gigs are hidden from artist search, but a malicious or stale client could still POST to `/api/gigs/{id}/slots/{sid}/book` directly. Without this guard, the booking would land but the hold rotation wouldn't advance and the venue's hold-accepted email wouldn't fire.
  5. **Pre-book gate ordering verified.** Audit reported gates ran AFTER the pay override write, which would leak a bumped slot.pay even when a gate failed. Verified: gates ARE before override (lines 207-303 → 305-341). False positive.
  6. **Blast helper door-deal gap documented.** `_get_effective_pay_for_artist` at routes/gigs.py:5917 takes only flat `base_pay` and has no slot/door-deal awareness. Acceptable for now since blasts pass `gig.pay` (gig-level) and door deals live at the slot level. Added a docstring note. Proper fix: slot-aware blast emails — out of scope.
  7. **`apply_override` toggle audit clean** — every booking, modal, banner, email surface respects the gate correctly per audit 1. No changes needed beyond the existing implementation.

- **2026-06-24 (Hold UX pass — apply_override toggle + auto-refresh + bubble color + slot counts):** Several customer-visible improvements from a test session.
  1. **Per-slot `apply_override` toggle for door deals.** Until today flat slots always honored the per-artist pay override but door slots NEVER did (the guarantee floor was final). The user wanted door deals to optionally honor the override per slot. New `gig_slots.apply_override INTEGER DEFAULT 0` column ([db.py](backend/db.py)). New checkbox on the door slot config UI: "Apply artist override" — default OFF so behavior matches the prior status quo. When checked, an artist with a per-venue override sees the override as the new guarantee floor (e.g. "$10 + 50% door" becomes "$15 + 50% door" for an artist with a $15 override at this venue). Backend gates this in four places: `_effective_pay_for_artist`, `book_gig`, `book_slot`, and `respond_to_hold_offer`. Frontend renders the effective amount from the API in both branches; both flat and door slot rendering simplified to use `s.pay` (the API now applies the override correctly).
  2. **Auto-refresh venue Pending Offers banner after creating a Hold gig.** Banner used to wait 60s for its next poll before showing the new held gig. Now both the create-gig flow (with hold) and the post-creation `/hold/start` flow call the exposed `window.refreshVenueHoldOffersBanner()` immediately after success.
  3. **Bubble color for booked-by-me on held gigs.** A held gig where one slot has been booked by THIS artist (other slots still in offer cycle) was showing as black/'started' for the booking artist — looked locked instead of "this is yours". Added an early-return in `getGigClass` that returns `'booked-mine'` when the viewing artist has a booked slot on a held gig.
  4. **Venue Pending Offers banner now shows slot counts.** New `open_slot_count` / `booked_slot_count` / `total_slot_count` fields on `/api/me/venue-hold-offers`. Banner renders a chip "X/Y booked · Z open" inline next to the gig date so the venue sees per-gig progress at a glance.
  5. **Close gig modal after Book click on Hold accept.** Previously the Pending Offer modal stayed open with a disabled '…' Book button + lingering Decline after a successful book; user had to manually dismiss. `_accept` now calls `window._gmClose()` on success before the banner refresh, mirroring the existing modal-close pattern.
  6. **DEFERRED:** Hold-accept routing through the contract-signing flow when the venue requires a contract. Significant cross-file integration (exposing contract modals from artist.book-gigs.js, generalizing book-with-contract to handle hold cleanup, threading the hold token through the signing flow). Tackled in the next pass.

- **2026-06-23 (Hold audit sweep — pre-book gates + override status guards + orphan-offer cleanup):** Findings from three parallel audit agents, verified against the actual code and fixed. Six concrete issues addressed; one audit finding (contract auto-exec in book_slot) confirmed as false positive — that path lives only in `approve_booking` (same-day venue approval) and `book_slot` itself doesn't create contracts either, so hold-accept is at parity.
  1. **Hold-accept missing pre-book gates.** `respond_to_hold_offer` ([services/gig_hold.py](backend/services/gig_hold.py)) was claiming the slot without running the safety gates `book_slot` runs first. Added three: (a) `_enforce_no_artist_time_overlap` — prevents an artist from accepting two overlapping holds; (b) venue frequency-days policy — mirrors `book_slot:4577-4606` query/waiver shape (holds do NOT waive frequency — a hold is an invitation, not a blast); (c) Stripe Connect onboarding gate — blocks accept with a clear message if the artist's payout account isn't set up, instead of letting the booking land + the transaction creation silently fail downstream. Each gate returns `{ok: False, message: ...}` so the accept landing page surfaces a useful error.
  2. **Pay-override missing `status='approved'` guards.** Four sites in [routes/gigs.py](backend/routes/gigs.py): (a) dead-code block at 2455 fetching override without status guard — removed; (b) live `book_gig` query at 2561 — guard added; (c) live `book_slot` query at 4631 — guard added; (d) `_get_effective_pay_for_artist` helper at 5872 used by blast emails — guard added. Without the guard, a venue who set $20 then revoked the relationship would still apply $20 at book time and show $20 in blast emails. Other override sites (`gig_modal.py`, `email_dispatch.py`, `contracts.py`, frontend) were already correctly guarded — audit verified.
  3. **`/api/gigs/{gid}/hold/start` allowed adding already-booked artists.** A venue could call `start_hold` with an artist already booked into another slot of this gig — that artist could then accept the hold email and end up booked into TWO slots of the same gig. The per-slot pre-book gates can't catch this since the second slot is genuinely open. Added an up-front check in `start_hold` that queries booked/pending statuses and rejects with a per-artist message naming who conflicts.
  4. **`/api/gigs/{gid}/hold/reorder` left orphan offer when removing the currently-offered artist.** Post-creation-added artists can become the currently-offered one once the original locked list is exhausted. Removing them via reorder DELETEd the waitlist row but the artist's email link (`/hold/respond/{token}`) stayed valid for the 24h window — they could click and book a slot the venue thought they'd been pulled from. Fix: before the DELETE, mark the offer declined (offer_declined=1, offer_expires_at=CURRENT_TIMESTAMP) so `respond_to_hold_offer`'s offer-state check rejects the click cleanly; then call `send_next_hold_offer` to advance the rotation. Idempotent — if no next eligible row exists this just marks the hold exhausted.

- **2026-06-23 (Book button UX + Hold-accept post-book pipeline + venue email pay):** Three fixes after a user-reported Book-button hang exposed deeper gaps in the Hold-accept code path.
  1. **Book button on the artist Pending-Offers banner appeared to do nothing.** Diagnosis: clicking Book opened the confirmation modal whose `onConfirm` was `() => _accept(...)` — an arrow returning the Promise from the async `_accept`. The gf-modals builder treats a returned Promise as "async, keep modal open". So the slot got booked server-side but the modal stayed open, the user clicked Cancel (which closed it but did nothing), then opened the gig modal and saw the gig was now booked — making it look like Cancel did the booking. Fix in [hold-offers-banner.js](app/static/js/hold-offers-banner.js) (`v=10`): wrap _accept in a body block `() => { _accept(...); }` so it returns undefined → modal closes immediately. Added a `showSuccessModal` "Booked!" toast on the success path so the artist gets explicit feedback.
  2. **Hold acceptance bypassed the full booking post-book pipeline.** `respond_to_hold_offer` was doing a slot UPDATE + venue-side notification only — missing: artist booking confirmation email, transaction row (no money would move), the in-app `notify_gig_booked` fanout to multi-user entity_users, and cancellation-waitlist cleanup. Added `_run_hold_post_book_pipeline` in [services/gig_hold.py](backend/services/gig_hold.py) that mirrors the tail of `book_slot`: waitlist cleanup, `notify_gig_booked`, hold-specific `_notify_venue_accept`, `send_booking_emails`, `_create_booking_transaction`. Each step is try/except so a downstream failure doesn't undo earlier work — same defensive pattern as the regular book path. Logs use a `[HOLD][TXN_MISSING]` tag on transaction failures so admin can spot + recover via the Payments console.
  3. **Hold-Accepted venue email rendered "$10" for an artist with a $15 override.** `_notify_venue_accept` was pulling `slot["pay"]` from the `_list_open_hold_slots` row — the RAW slot pay, pre-override (the override is only applied at booking time, not in the read query). Added a `pay_display` template variable that uses door-aware formatting (`"$X + Y% door"` for door slots, `"$N"` for flat) and that pulls from the slot's pay column AFTER my new hold-accept booking-time override bump has run. Updated [email_templates.py](backend/email_templates.py) `hold_accept_venue` body to use `{{pay_display}}` instead of `${{pay}}`. Note: the user reported this as a door-deal issue, but the DB confirms the slot is flat — the $10/$15 confusion came from the per-artist override (Fifty Proof has a $15 override at venue 1 against a $10 published slot). The pay_display fix renders the correct effective amount regardless.

- **2026-06-23 (banner Cancel auto-refresh + add Hold to existing gigs):** Two follow-ups to the venue-side Hold polish.
  1. **Banner Cancel Gig now auto-refreshes the calendar.** After clicking Cancel Gig in the venue Pending Offers banner the page still showed the gig until manual reload. The banner now (a) closes any open `#gigModal`, (b) calls the newly-exposed `window.invalidateGigs()` to flush the venue.create-gigs cache, then (c) calls `window.refreshGigs()`. Same wiring for Open-to-All. Cache: `venue-hold-offers-banner.js?v=3`, `venue.create-gigs.js?v=204`.
  2. **Hold Gig pill now enables on existing gigs.** Previously the pill was greyed in the Edit Gig modal because `_updateHoldPillState` only fired on input events — slot rows populated programmatically by `addSlotRow` during edit-modal open didn't trigger it. Added a MutationObserver on `#slotList` so the pill state recomputes whenever rows are added/removed. Pill is hidden entirely when the gig is already held (the existing hold-mgmt panel is the surface there). Save flow now POSTs to the new `/api/gigs/{gig_id}/hold/start` endpoint when Hold is toggled ON during edit and the gig wasn't already held. Backend endpoint validates: not already held, status ≠ booked/cancelled, ≥1 open slot, non-empty artist list — then delegates to the existing `create_hold_waitlist` service. Cache: `venue.create-gigs.js?v=205`.

- **2026-06-23 (booking-flow audit fixes — hold acceptance override + modal door-deal skip):** Two bugs found in a focused audit of the booking + pay-override path across single-slot / multi-slot / flat / door / hold gig combinations.
  1. **Hold-offer acceptance was bypassing the per-artist pay override.** When an artist accepted a Hold offer (via email or in-app banner), `respond_to_hold_offer` in [backend/services/gig_hold.py](backend/services/gig_hold.py) booked the slot via a plain `UPDATE gig_slots SET artist_id=..., status='booked'` without first applying the override. Net effect: the artist saw the override-adjusted pay in the offer email + modal banner, accepted, and then the slot got recorded at the published (lower) pay — the venue would be undercharged and the artist underpaid. Added the same override-then-book sequence already in `book_slot` ([routes/gigs.py:2557](backend/routes/gigs.py#L2557)). Door-deal slots are skipped (guarantee floor is final).
  2. **Gig modal applied override to door-deal slots.** [backend/routes/gig_modal.py:265-280](backend/routes/gig_modal.py#L265) iterated every slot and bumped `_s["pay"]` if an override existed, regardless of `deal_type`. Door slots should show "$guarantee + door%" verbatim — the override is the per-gig opt-out. Other three places (booking endpoints, hold-offer email, banner) all correctly skipped door slots; the modal was the odd one out. Added the `if _s.get("deal_type") == "door": continue` guard.
  3. Audit also verified that held gigs are correctly excluded from `/api/gigs/public`, blast emails (36h/1w/4w/radius), and cancellation-waitlist offer flow — no regressions in the gating that hides held gigs from public discovery.

- **2026-06-23 (Hold polish — venue Pending Offers banner + pay override + offeree blink):** Three customer-visible improvements to the Hold-Gig feature.
  0. **Venue-side Pending Offers banner** at the top of `app/venue-create-gigs.html`. Mirrors the artist banner but from the venue perspective so the venue user gets a live snapshot of every held gig: who's currently up on the clock, how long they have left, how many queued/declined behind them. New endpoint `GET /api/me/venue-hold-offers` ([routes/gigs.py](backend/routes/gigs.py)) aggregates `gigs.hold_status IN ('active','exhausted')` across every venue the user can manage (primary + `entity_users`), with per-gig waitlist counts derived from `gig_waitlist`. New JS file `app/static/js/venue-hold-offers-banner.js` (v=1) polls every 60s + on tab focus. **Exhausted gigs flash a red pulsing border** (via the `vhob-flash` keyframe) and gain two action buttons — "Open to All" (`POST /api/gigs/{gid}/hold/resolve-exhausted` with `{action:'open_all'}`) and "Cancel Gig" (`DELETE /api/gigs/{gid}/with-slots` — the standard venue-UI cancel path per the three-cancellation-endpoints note in CLAUDE.md). The banner sorts exhausted-first so action-needed gigs jump to the top. `renderCalendar` in venue.create-gigs.js is now exposed as `window.refreshGigs` so the banner can refresh the calendar after a successful action.
  1. **Pay-override propagation across all Hold-feature Pay displays.** The standing per-artist override on `preferred_artists.pay_dollars_override` was applied at booking time but NOT in the offer surfaces, so a venue who set a higher pay for Fifty Proof saw the base slot pay everywhere (banner, modal Pending Offer panel, email, slot-picker landing). Added `_effective_pay_for_artist(db, slot, venue_id, artist_id)` in `backend/services/gig_hold.py` — flat slots return `max(slot.pay, override)`, door slots return `guarantee_cents/100` verbatim (door split is the per-gig opt-out from standing override, mirroring the existing booking-time rule at `routes/gigs.py:2557`). Wired into: `/api/me/hold-offers` (banner), `/api/gigs/{id}/modal-data.hold_info.my_matching_slots` (gig modal Pending Offer), `_send_hold_offer_email` (email — flat + door rendering), and the multi-slot picker page at `/hold/respond/{token}` (precompute then render).
  2. **Offeree's calendar bubble blinks purple instead of staying black.** Held gigs return class `'started'` so the bubble looks locked to queued/declined artists. But the artist currently up on the clock was also seeing flat black — they needed a visual cue that THEY needed to act. Added a `gigClass === 'started' && holdOfferGigIds.has(g.id)` branch in `app/static/js/artist.book-gigs.js` (bumped to `v=162`) that paints a purple `linear-gradient(135deg, #7c6bff, #6052e0)` blink + tooltip "🎯 You're up — open to accept or decline this offer". Mirrors the banner color so the two surfaces feel cohesive.

- **2026-06-17 (five new features — backend MVPs) — iCal feed + bidirectional reviews + gig templates + SMS re-enabled + door deals:**
  1. **iCal calendar feed** ([routes/calendar_feed.py](backend/routes/calendar_feed.py)). Token-gated subscribable feed at `GET /api/calendar/{token}.ics`. Token IS the auth — `users.calendar_token` column (random UUID minted on first call to `/api/me/calendar-feed-url`). Returns booked + awaiting-venue-contract gigs for the user across every artist + venue they own (incl. multi-user via `entity_users`). UI on user-profile → Notifications: Calendar Sync card with copy + rotate buttons + Google/Apple/Outlook subscribe instructions. Complements the existing one-shot artist/venue download endpoints in gigs.py.
  2. **Bidirectional reviews** (artist → venue). 5 new endpoints in [routes/reviews.py](backend/routes/reviews.py): `POST /api/artists/{aid}/gigs/{gid}/review`, `POST /api/artists/{aid}/venues/{vid}/review`, `GET /api/venues/{vid}/reviews`, `GET /api/venues/{vid}/reviews/summary`, `GET /api/artists/{aid}/reviews/pending-venues`. `venue_reviews` table was already in db.py (May 2026); added `venues.avg_rating` + `venues.review_count` columns, plus a `_recompute_venue_rating()` helper that fires after every write to keep the aggregate in sync. FE surfacing (review CTA on past gigs + venue-profile reviews tab) is follow-up — mirrors the existing artist-review patterns.
  3. **Gig templates** ([routes/gig_templates.py](backend/routes/gig_templates.py)). New `gig_templates` table (`venue_id, name, slots_json`). CRUD on `/api/venues/{vid}/gig-templates`. `slots_json` is opaque to the backend — same shape the FE sends to gig-create, so applying a template = passing the parsed JSON into the existing create flow. Caps: 50 templates/venue, 20 slots/template, 120-char name. FE on Create Gig modal is follow-up.
  4. **SMS notifications re-enabled** ([email_service.py](backend/email_service.py)). The carrier-gateway pipeline ([sms_service.py](backend/sms_service.py), `sms_preferences` table, `/api/sms-carriers`, `/api/user-sms-preferences`) was already fully built but `_dispatch_sms` was no-op'd because carrier gateways were deemed unreliable. Re-enabled with honest UI framing: smsSetupSection in [user-profile.html](app/user-profile.html) shown with an EXPERIMENTAL badge and an italic carrier-reliability disclaimer (T-Mobile / Google Fi reliable, AT&T / Verizon filter aggressively). Twilio integration remains on the roadmap. Also added `users.sms_notifications_enabled` column for a future master toggle.
  5. **Door deal / split-pay settlement** ([routes/door_settle.py](backend/routes/door_settle.py)). New columns on `gig_slots`: `deal_type` ('flat'|'door'), `door_pct` (0-100), `guarantee_cents`, `door_receipts_cents`, `settled_pay_cents`, `settled_at`. Three endpoints:
     - `PUT  /api/gigs/{gid}/slots/{sid}/deal`           — venue marks a slot as a door deal + sets % and guarantee
     - `POST /api/gigs/{gid}/slots/{sid}/settle`         — venue files door receipts after the show; backend computes `max(guarantee, guarantee + receipts × pct/100)` and updates the scheduled `transactions` row in place. Only `status='scheduled'` rows are touched — anything charged or in flight is left alone.
     - `GET  /api/gigs/{gid}/slots/pending-settlement`   — list of door-deal slots awaiting settlement.
     Doesn't mutate `book_slot()` or the gig-create INSERT sites — venues opt slots into door-deal mode via the dedicated configure endpoint, keeping the existing booking + payout invariants intact.
  Test suite: still 37/37 passing after all five additions. No FE was migrated for #2, #3, #5; the calendar UI for #1 and the SMS UI for #4 (existing surfaced) are live. The other FE surfaces are the obvious follow-up batch.

- **2026-06-17 (deep audit fixes) — File ownership + 3 backend bugs + 2 UX gaps + test conftest catch-up:**
  1. **chown www-data:www-data on `backend/email_templates.py`, `db.py`, `email_service.py`.** Per CLAUDE.md, admin email-template edits write to DB *and* tries to write the file from the PUT endpoint at [admin.py:915](backend/routes/admin.py#L915). When the file was root-owned, the file write silently failed and changes were lost at the next deploy when `_populate_email_templates()` reloaded from disk. Verified: file now owned by www-data, edit + restart round-trip works.
  2. **`sign_contract()` type-check bug** ([contracts.py:935](backend/routes/contracts.py#L935)). The guard compared `contract_type` to the literal string `"digital"` — which is not a value the schema ever produces (`CHECK` constraint at [contracts.py:471](backend/routes/contracts.py#L471) restricts it to `pdf_upload | custom_builder | auto_generated`). Net effect: the gate fired for *every* typed contract and silently blocked `custom_builder` + `auto_generated` from ever being signed digitally via this endpoint. Inverted the check to block ONLY `pdf_upload`. The vulnerability path is now closed AND the legitimate digital-sign paths work.
  3. **`charge_booking()` INSERT missing `artist_id` + `transaction_type`** ([stripe_connect.py:628](backend/routes/stripe_connect.py#L628)). Transactions written by this endpoint omitted both fields, leaving `_transfer_to_artists()` (payout_scheduler) to fall back to legacy artist-routing which could pay the wrong artist on multi-user accounts. Both columns now bound explicitly (`'single'` for `transaction_type`).
  4. **Auth guard no longer shows a 12s blank page on slow networks** ([auth.guard.js](app/static/js/auth.guard.js)). The pre-paint script sets `visibility:hidden` on `<html>` to prevent content flash; if `/api/me` hangs, the existing 12s timeout would silently leave that hidden until the redirect fired. Added a small "Checking session…" overlay (purple spinner + label) that appears after 600ms if auth hasn't resolved, with `visibility:visible !important` to break out of the inherited hidden state. Removed on success/fail/timeout.
  5. **High-traffic raw `fetch()` error swallows migrated to `apiPostSafe`** in [messages.js](app/static/js/messages.js) (load + send) and [user-dropdown.js](app/static/js/user-dropdown.js) (Help + Feedback ticket submissions). Users now see the real backend `detail` ("Venue access revoked", "Frequency limit reached", etc.) in inline status text or a toast — instead of `"Failed to send"`. Total raw-fetch error swallows site-wide remain at ~118, migration can continue incrementally as bugs surface.
  6. **`_findNextBlast()` filter inversion** ([venue.create-gigs.js](app/static/js/venue.create-gigs.js)). The banner on a gig that had already fired a 2-week blast always said "No further automated blast emails are scheduled" even when 1-week / 36-hour notices were enabled. The filter required `t.wh > _firedH` ("only blasts with LARGER threshold than what already fired") — but blasts fire in *decreasing* threshold order (4w → 2w → 1w → 36h), so larger means already-fired. Flipped to `t.wh < _firedH`. Banner now correctly says e.g. "Next scheduled blast: 1 week notice — will fire automatically if this gig is still open." Also widened the `_lbl()` formatter to render `36 hours` instead of `36h`.
  7. **Test conftest catch-up** ([tests/conftest.py](tests/conftest.py)). 17 tests were failing because the in-memory SQLite schema had drifted from production. Added missing columns (`users.phone`, `users.sms_carrier`, `transactions.parent_transaction_id`, `transactions.transaction_type`, `gig_contracts.signed_pdf_path` + 8 others), and added six missing satellite tables touched by `gig_cleanup` (`gig_messages`, `gig_email_log`, `public_activity`, `gig_waitlist`, `waitlist_offered`, `artist_reviews`). Full test suite: **37/37 passing** (was 20/37).
  8. **Audit finding `_cleanup_expired_holds_impl` "orphaned payment transaction" closed as NOT-A-BUG** — the May 2026 part 5 audit fix intentionally defers `_create_booking_transaction()` to `countersign_contract()` so that if the venue ghosts within 48h, no charge is ever taken. Confirmed by reading both [contracts.py:1011-1017](backend/routes/contracts.py#L1011) and [contracts.py:1667](backend/routes/contracts.py#L1667). The flow is consistent: artist-sign-only → no transaction, both-sign → transaction; expiry sweep has nothing to clean up because there's nothing to clean up.

- **2026-06-16 (social reorder save bug + button-width parity) — Two small follow-ups:**
  1. **Social reorder wasn't persisting** on artist edit. The dragend handler PUT to `/api/artists/{id}` — but the artist update endpoint is `/artists/{id}` (no `/api` prefix). The `/api/artists/{id}` path is a GET-only public route, so the PUT was silently 405'd and the save dropped on the floor. Verified: old URL → 405, new URL `/artists/{id}` → 401 unauth (endpoint exists). Switched the fetch to `/artists/${artistId}` to match the `bindAutosave` pattern already used elsewhere in the same file. Venue side was already correct (`/api/venues/{id}` is a real PUT route there).
  2. **"+ Add MP3 File" and "+ Add Audio Link" buttons now have matching widths** (170px min-width on both, centered text). Without the min-width, the MP3 button's text changed length as the count badge ("(2/3)") updated, and the two buttons sat at different widths. 170px fits the longest state ("+ Add MP3 File (2/3)") with comfortable padding and matches "+ Add Audio Link" exactly. Cache-buster: `artist.edit.js?v=15`.

- **2026-06-16 (Reviews badge + MP3 layout + sortable Social Media) — Three polish items:**
  1. **Reviews tab on artist public profile now shows count badge** ("Reviews (1)") like the venue tab does. Added `<span id="reviewsBadge">` to the tab button, fetched the summary once on page load to populate it eagerly, and updated `renderRatingSummary` in [artist-reviews.js](app/static/js/artist-reviews.js) to also set the badge when the user clicks the tab (covers both paths).
  2. **Audio section layout** on artist-edit: the "+ Add MP3 File" button now sits on the same line as the help text, right-aligned, so it vertically lines up with the "+ Add Audio Link" button on the row below. Wrapped both in a flexbox; help text gets `flex:1`, button gets `flex-shrink:0`.
  3. **Social media is now drag-sortable** on both artist and venue edit pages — and the chosen order applies to the public profile. New `social_order TEXT` column on `artists` and `venues` (comma-separated brand keys), migrated live + added to [db.py](backend/db.py) `_add_columns` for fresh installs + ORM models. Backend GET endpoints (`/api/artists/{id}`, `/api/artists/{id}/public`, `/api/venues/{id}/public`, plus the auth `/api/{id}` for artists) return it; PUT endpoints accept it via COALESCE so it can be updated independently of the URL fields. Edit pages: each `.social-row` got a `<span class="drag-handle">☰</span>` and `data-brand="..."`; new `setupSocialReorder(artistId)` / `setupVenueSocialReorder(venueId)` helpers wire drag-to-reorder on `#socialGrid`; on `dragend` the brand keys in current DOM order are joined and PUT to the artist/venue endpoint. On load, the saved order rearranges DOM rows before drag wiring fires. Public profiles ([artist-profile.html](app/artist-profile.html), [venue-profile.html](app/venue-profile.html)) read `social_order` and sort the `links[]` array before rendering; brands not in the saved order keep their natural position relative to each other at the end (so a user adding a new social field later just appends).
  - Cache-busters bumped: `artist.edit.js?v=14`, `venue.edit.js?v=6`. API restarted to pick up the new GET response field + PUT param.
  - End-to-end verified: setting `social_order='instagram,spotify,facebook,website'` on Fifty Proof persists, returns via `/api/artists/3`, surfaces on the rendered HTML via `/fiftyproof`. Same on `/api/venues/1/public` and `/14cannons`.

- **2026-06-16 (social tiles) — Branded social-media grid replaces emoji link list.** The social media tab on both public profiles was a left-aligned vertical list of pill buttons with emoji icons (🎵 Spotify, 📷 Instagram, etc.). Replaced with a **centered grid of branded tiles**, mirroring the new video/picture grid pattern:
  - Each tile is a 110px square card with the brand's real **inline SVG logo** above the brand name. Single-color paths so the icon tints to the brand's iconic color (Spotify green, Instagram pink, Facebook blue, YouTube red, X mono, TikTok cyan, Yelp red, Google Maps red, Website cyan).
  - Hover lifts the tile, scales the icon by 1.1×, and shifts the border to the brand color via `--brand` CSS variable set inline per-tile.
  - Grid uses `repeat(auto-fit, 110px) + justify-content: center` so a row with 3 of 6 visible (the common case for venues — many don't fill all 6 social fields) renders centered, not left-aligned.
  - Mobile breakpoint (<480px): tiles shrink to 90px, icons 28px.
  - **No external dependencies** — all 9 SVG paths are embedded inline (~2.5kb total). No Font Awesome, no CDN, no extra HTTP request. Brand keys: spotify, instagram, facebook, youtube, twitter, tiktok, website, yelp, google_maps. Helper `brandIcon(name)` + map `BRAND_COLORS{name: hex}` defined at the top of each page's script block.
  - Same component on both [artist-profile.html](app/artist-profile.html) and [venue-profile.html](app/venue-profile.html). Verified each SVG parses (viewBox + path with d-length 100+ chars).

- **2026-06-16 (pictures + centered grids + centered tabs) — Symmetric polish on both public profiles.** Three small changes that make the whole page look balanced:
  1. **Centered video + picture grids.** Switched from `grid-template-columns: repeat(4, 1fr)` (which always left-aligned partial rows) to `repeat(auto-fit, 210px)` + `justify-content: center`. Now a row with 3 of 4 columns full renders centered, and a row with just 1 card sits in the middle of the container. Cleanest grid-native approach — no flex/wrap tricks needed.
  2. **Centered header tabs.** Added `justify-content: center` to `.profile-tabs` on both [artist-profile.html](app/artist-profile.html) and [venue-profile.html](app/venue-profile.html). Tabs center when they all fit; horizontal scroll still kicks in on narrow viewports where content overflows.
  3. **Pictures get the video treatment.** Same 4-per-row 210px cards, same 130px-tall thumbnails, same big title + multi-line caption row. Merged `#videos.media-grid` and `#pictures.media-grid` into one shared rule. Picture caption field added to both edit pages: `<textarea class="picture-caption">` inside the existing card overlay, autosaves on blur, Enter commits (no newline). New `.picture-caption` class added to the shared keydown/blur handler matchers in [artist.edit.js](app/static/js/artist.edit.js) and [venue.edit.js](app/static/js/venue.edit.js). Public profiles render the caption only when non-empty — plain pictures look identical to before this change.
  - End-to-end verified: picture caption persists to DB, returns via public GET, surfaces on the rendered card (sample: "Backstage at the Echo · Sept 9 2026" round-tripped through `gigsfill.com/fridayspast`).
  - Cache-busters: `artist.edit.js?v=13`, `venue.edit.js?v=5`. No backend restart needed.

- **2026-06-16 (video grid bigger) — Responsive 4-column video grid with room for longer captions.** The pictures grid stays as-is (quantity-first gallery); videos get bigger cards because captions were truncating at 2 lines in the narrow 120-px-wide cells. Added `#videos.media-grid` override on both [artist-profile.html](app/artist-profile.html) and [venue-profile.html](app/venue-profile.html):
  - 4 cols on desktop (>1000px) → 3 cols (700-1000px) → 2 cols (480-700px) → 1 full-width col (<480px, with a taller 180px thumbnail at that size since it's the only one on screen).
  - Thumbnails: 130px tall by default (consistent height for the row).
  - Title: bumped to 0.78rem, **bold**, allowed to wrap to 2 lines, left-aligned padding kept centered text.
  - Caption: bumped to 0.72rem, left-aligned, allowed up to **5 lines** before ellipsis. Background made transparent so caption visually belongs to the card body rather than an isolated dark strip.
  - Selector specificity (`#videos.media-grid` is id+class) cleanly overrides the existing `.media-grid` mobile rule without modifying that rule, so the pictures gallery layout is unchanged.

- **2026-06-16 (video captions) — Per-video caption on artist + venue profiles.** Same `caption` column we added for audio (already on `artist_media` and `venue_media`); just wired up for `media_type='video'` rows on all four media-handling pages. No new schema, no new endpoints — the `/api/media/{id}` (artist) and `/api/venues/media/{id}` (venue) PUTs already accept any column via `hasattr(m, k)`, and the GET responses already include `caption`.
  - **Edit pages** — caption sits inside the existing `.media-overlay` block below the title input. Full-width `<textarea class="video-caption">` with placeholder "Add a caption (e.g. Live at the Roxy · Aug 14)…", max 500 chars, Enter commits (blurs → save) so it behaves like a one-line label. Same shared handler also covers `.audio-caption` and `.media-title`. Added `escapeHtml` helper to venue.edit.js (artist.edit.js already had one) so a user-typed `</textarea>` can't break out.
  - **Public profiles** — caption rendered as `<div class="media-caption-label">` *below* the existing title label, only when non-empty. Plain videos look identical to before this change (no blank row). 2-line max via `-webkit-line-clamp` so a long caption doesn't blow up the grid cell — full text shows in the modal player's title bar if needed.
  - **Verified end-to-end**: set caption on a Fridays Past video → `/api/artists/1/media` returns it → vanity URL `/fridayspast` serves HTML containing the new render code → empty caption returns null and frontend correctly skips the row.
  - Cache-busters: `artist.edit.js?v=12`, `venue.edit.js?v=4`. No backend restart needed (no Python changes).

- **2026-06-16 (vanity HTML cache fix + MP3-only) — Root cause of "edits don't show up on gigsfill.com/bandname".** Two related fixes:
  1. **The vanity URL resolver was caching `artist-profile.html` / `venue-profile.html` in-memory forever.** `_load_profile_html()` in [vanity.py](backend/routes/vanity.py) used a simple `{name: contents}` dict that only invalidated on API restart. Effect: any edit to those HTML files wasn't visible at `gigsfill.com/<slug>` until `sudo systemctl restart gigsfill`. This presented to the user as "the audio caption isn't showing on the public profile" and "I reordered tracks but the public page still shows the old order" — both looked like browser-side caching but were actually server-side. **Fix:** swapped to `{name: (mtime_ns, contents)}` with an `os.stat()` mtime check on each request. Cache hit when file unchanged; cache miss + re-read when file mtime advances. One extra `stat()` per request, no real perf cost.
  2. **Audio uploads are now MP3-only** (was: mp3/wav/m4a/ogg/flac/aac). Tightened `ALLOWED_EXTENSIONS['audio']` and `ALLOWED_MIME_TYPES` in [routes/media.py](backend/routes/media.py), tightened the file picker's `accept="audio/mpeg,.mp3"` on artist-edit.html, and added a client-side extension check that fires **before** the size check so a `.wav` upload gets a clear "MP3 only" message immediately. Backend still enforces the format check defensively. Users with non-MP3 files can convert or use the existing "+ Add Audio Link" field for external hosting.
  - Service worker `CACHE_NAME` bumped `gigsfill-v6 → gigsfill-v7` to force eviction of any client-side cached HTML that pre-dates the audio-entry rewrite.
  - Verified end-to-end: `gigsfill.com/fridayspast` now serves HTML containing `audio-caption-display` + `audio-entry` (9 references; previously 0); reorder roundtrip persists to DB and GET returns rows in the new order. API restarted to load the fixed vanity cache.

- **2026-06-16 (audio caption polish) — Enter commits + public profile shows caption.** Three small follow-ups to the audio-caption feature:
  - **Enter in the caption now commits** (blurs → save). The previous build used Ctrl/Cmd+Enter because the field is a textarea (Enter would naturally insert a newline). User feedback: captions are short labels (e.g. "Live at the Roxy · Aug 14, 2026"), not paragraphs, so single-line behavior is the right default. `preventDefault` stops the newline.
  - **Blur autosave was already working** — verified end-to-end against a real row the user had captioned during testing ("This is Q1 song...Type anything here." round-tripped via the public GET).
  - **Caption now displays on artist public profile** ([artist-profile.html](app/artist-profile.html)) — wrapped each audio row in a `.audio-entry` div with a `.audio-caption-display` above the player, rendered **only when caption is non-empty**. Empty captions leave the layout looking identical to the pre-feature version (no blank row). Both `audio` and `audio_link` media types covered.
  - Cache-buster: `artist.edit.js?v=11`.

- **2026-06-16 — Audio uploads on artist-edit: 5 MB cap + per-track caption.** Two related changes to the Audio section:
  - **5 MB size cap** (was 50 MB). Lowered `MAX_FILE_SIZES['audio']` in [routes/media.py](backend/routes/media.py); enough for a ~5-minute 128 kbps demo clip. UI text now reads "Upload up to **3 MP3 files** (max **5 MB each**)". Client-side check in [artist.edit.js](app/static/js/artist.edit.js) catches oversize files before upload and shows: "That file is X MB — the limit is 5 MB. Try re-encoding at a lower bitrate (128 kbps is plenty for a demo clip), trimming the track, or linking to it on SoundCloud/Bandcamp instead." Backend still enforces in case the check is bypassed.
  - **Per-track caption textarea above each audio row.** New `caption TEXT` column on both `artist_media` and `venue_media` (migrated live + added to `db.py` CREATE TABLE + ORM models so the existing `/api/media/{id}` PUT accepts it via `hasattr(m, k)`). GET responses now include `caption`. The audio render in `artist.edit.js` wraps each row in a new `.audio-entry` card containing the full-width textarea on top + the existing (drag-handle, title, audio player, Delete) row below. Saves on blur (mirror of the title-save handler); Ctrl/Cmd+Enter commits without inserting a newline. Drag-reorder + delete updated to target `.audio-entry` so the caption moves/deletes with its row.
  - Cache-buster: `artist.edit.js?v=10`. API restarted to pick up the new MAX size + GET-response field.

- **2026-06-16 — artist-profile.html tab underline bug: Pictures and Videos were swapped.** `switchTab(name)` highlights the active button via `btns[TAB_NAMES.indexOf(name)]`, but `TAB_NAMES` had `videos` BEFORE `pictures` while the actual button DOM had Pictures before Videos. Result: clicking Pictures correctly opened the Pictures panel but the underline appeared under the Videos button (and vice versa). Reads as "contents not always correct" because users use the underline to tell which tab is active. One-line fix: reordered `TAB_NAMES` to match button DOM order (`info, calendar, pictures, videos, audio, social, reviews`). Verified all 7 tabs now light up the matching button. Added a comment warning future edits to keep the two in sync. Venue-profile.html uses the same pattern but its array+DOM already match, so no bug there.

- **2026-06-14 (part 10p audit) — Multi-agent audit of today's invitation feature surfaced 10 real bugs (out of ~30 reported); all fixed.** Spawned 3 parallel Explore agents over backend Phase 1, backend Phase 2/3, frontend, and cross-cutting code. Triaged false positives directly (no UNIQUE constraint needed on token: 256-bit entropy ⇒ collision-free; LIMIT 1 on artist lookup: data model implies one artist per user; `_pendingInviteHookFired` doesn't persist across navigation; etc.). The real findings, and what shipped:
  1. **`db.py` was missing the `artist_invitations` indexes on token / invite_group_id / LOWER(invited_email)** — the migration created them on the live DB via ALTER, but a fresh install from `setup_database()` would have skipped them. Added `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` statements after the `CREATE TABLE` block. By-token lookups now scale.
  2. **The decline endpoint was a state-mutating GET** — email-link prefetchers (Outlook safe-link checking, antivirus URL scanners, link previewers) trigger automatic GET requests on every URL in an inbox, so invitations could be marked `declined` without the invitee ever clicking. Split into two endpoints: `GET /api/invitations/{token}/decline` now returns a confirmation page with a button that fires `POST /api/invitations/{token}/decline`. The POST does the actual state change. Prefetchers can never auto-decline.
  3. **The decline state machine guard was missing `bounced` and `expired`** — a row could theoretically be flipped from `bounced` → `declined`. Added both to the `NOT IN (…)` clause; verified with a unit test that all six terminal states (`signed_up`, `preferred_requested`, `preferred_approved`, `preferred_denied`, `declined`, `bounced`, `expired`) are correctly preserved.
  4. **DSN fallback regex could mark our own platform_email as bounced** — the body-text fallback pattern in [`scheduler.py:_parse_dsn_failed_recipients`](backend/scheduler.py) was greedy. If a DSN quoted back our own From header (which is common) the regex matched the OUR address near the "550" code. Added an exclusion set built from the DSN's `From` / `Reply-To` / `Return-Path` / `Sender` / `Errors-To` headers plus daemon-address prefixes (`mailer-daemon@`, `postmaster@`, `noreply@`, `no-reply@`). Verified end-to-end: a synthetic bounce that names `noreply@gigsfill.com` in the body produces zero matches.
  5. **Bounce check was overwriting `signed_up` rows with `bounced`** — the SQL filter `status IN ('pending', 'signed_up')` would clobber a successful signup if a late DSN arrived for the original invite email weeks later. Tightened to `status = 'pending'` only — once the invitee has moved past pending, the original invite's deliverability is irrelevant.
  6. **Resend cooldown error was hidden in `title` attribute** — when [`POST /api/artist-invitations/{token}/resend`](backend/routes/me.py) returned a 429 with "Please wait about 21h 4m before resending…", the My Invites page only set `btn.title = e.message` (browser tooltip on hover). Users wouldn't see the cooldown duration without hovering. Now surfaces an inline `⚠ …` red box below the card; 429 specifically resets the button to its default state so the user can retry later.
  7. **Invite POST committed only at the end of the batch** — if a DB exception fired mid-iteration (after emails had already been sent), all prior rows would roll back even though the recipients had received the email. Now commits per email (with rollback-on-failure logging). Partial success is preserved.
  8. **Bounce-Detection "Test & Poll Now" button raced with debounced autosave** — admin could toggle the enable checkbox and click Test immediately; the server would see the not-yet-saved state and return `reason: 'disabled'`. Fixed by awaiting `window.autoSaveSettings()` before the POST.
  9. **Email count >50 turned RED** in the invite modal — but the user explicitly removed the 50-email cap, so red implied a limit that doesn't exist. Switched to amber (`#f59e0b`) so the color reads as "double-check" instead of "blocked".
  10. **`_save_bounce_check_result` UPDATE-then-INSERT race** — concurrent run-now calls could both reach INSERT and the loser would crash on the `setting_key` UNIQUE constraint. Wrapped INSERT in try/except → UPDATE fallback, mirroring the pattern used in `admin.py:update_settings`.
